```python
def test_plant_grows_when_water_positive():
    g = _garden()
    g.run(15)
    for sid, p in g.plants().items():
        assert len(p.records()) > 0


def test_plant_refuses_when_own_water_zero():
    plants = [
        PlantAssignment("a", (4, 10, 0),
                        {"branch_length": 2, "max_depth": 2,
                         "water": 0, "light": 10}),
    ]
    template = GardenTemplate(
        template_id="t",
        slot_rects=[SlotRect("a", 1, 3, 8, 8)],
        water_width=12, water_height=12,
        rain_rate=128, evap_rate=10,
        energy_cap=30, growth_cost=1,
        branch_policy=None)
    g = FractalGarden(template, plants, _kp())
    g.run(15)
    p = g.plants()["a"]
    # No growth records, despite water being available
    assert len(p.records()) == 0
    # Energy was still delivered (soak happens regardless)
    assert g.energy("a") > 0


# -- Log and replay --

def test_tick_chain():
    g = _garden()
    g.run(5)
    recs = g.records()
    assert len(recs) == 5
    assert recs[0].prev is None
    for i in range(1, len(recs)):
        assert recs[i].prev == recs[i - 1].record_hash()


def test_log_verifies():
    g = _garden()
    g.run(10)
    ok, r = verify_garden_log(g)
    assert ok, r


def test_replay_verifies():
    g = _garden()
    g.run(10)
    ok, r = verify_garden_replay(g)
    assert ok, r


def test_plants_log_verifies():
    g = _garden()
    g.run(15)
    for sid, p in g.plants().items():
        ok, r = verify_world_log(p)
        assert ok, r
        ok, r = verify_growth(p)
        assert ok, r


# -- Branch refusals --

def test_refuse_observe_tier():
    g = _garden()
    g.run(5)
    bob_kp = _kp()
    bob_key = g.issue_world_key(
        _did_from_kp(bob_kp), "observe")
    req = sign_garden_branch_request(
        g.world_id, g.last_hash(),
        {"light_level": 3}, bob_kp, bob_key)
    r = g.request_branch(req)
    assert isinstance(r, GardenRefusalRecord)
    assert r.reason == "insufficient_tier"


def test_refuse_sandbox():
    g = _garden(template=_template(branchy=False))
    g.run(5)
    alice_kp = _kp()
    alice_key = g.issue_world_key(
        _did_from_kp(alice_kp), "terminal")
    req = sign_garden_branch_request(
        g.world_id, g.last_hash(),
        {"light_level": 3}, alice_kp, alice_key)
    r = g.request_branch(req)
    assert isinstance(r, GardenRefusalRecord)
    assert r.reason == "sandboxed"


def test_refuse_out_of_range():
    g = _garden()
    g.run(5)
    alice_kp = _kp()
    alice_key = g.issue_world_key(
        _did_from_kp(alice_kp), "control",
        param_scopes={"light_level": (3, 10)})
    req = sign_garden_branch_request(
        g.world_id, g.last_hash(),
        {"light_level": 99}, alice_kp, alice_key)
    r = g.request_branch(req)
    assert isinstance(r, GardenRefusalRecord)
    assert r.reason == "template_range:light_level"


def test_refuse_unknown_param():
    g = _garden()
    g.run(5)
    alice_kp = _kp()
    alice_key = g.issue_world_key(
        _did_from_kp(alice_kp), "control")
    req = sign_garden_branch_request(
        g.world_id, g.last_hash(),
        {"nonsense": 3}, alice_kp, alice_key)
    r = g.request_branch(req)
    assert isinstance(r, GardenRefusalRecord)
    assert r.reason == "unknown_param:nonsense"


def test_refusal_is_in_log():
    g = _garden()
    g.run(5)
    bob_kp = _kp()
    bob_key = g.issue_world_key(
        _did_from_kp(bob_kp), "observe")
    req = sign_garden_branch_request(
        g.world_id, g.last_hash(),
        {"light_level": 3}, bob_kp, bob_key)
    g.request_branch(req)
    refusals = [r for r in g.records()
                if isinstance(r, GardenRefusalRecord)]
    assert len(refusals) == 1
    assert refusals[0].requester_id == _did_from_kp(bob_kp)


# -- Branch acceptance --

def test_accept_branch():
    g = _garden()
    g.run(8)
    alice_kp = _kp()
    alice_key = g.issue_world_key(
        _did_from_kp(alice_kp), "control",
        param_scopes={"light_level": (3, 10)})
    req = sign_garden_branch_request(
        g.world_id, g.last_hash(),
        {"light_level": 3}, alice_kp, alice_key)
    r = g.request_branch(req)
    assert isinstance(r, tuple)
    seed, branch = r
    assert branch.template.light_level == 3
    # Parent's template unchanged
    assert g.template.light_level == 10


def test_branch_inherits_water_and_plants():
    g = _garden()
    g.run(8)
    parent_water = [list(row) for row in g.grid.levels]
    alice_kp = _kp()
    alice_key = g.issue_world_key(
        _did_from_kp(alice_kp), "control")
    req = sign_garden_branch_request(
        g.world_id, g.last_hash(),
        {"light_level": 3}, alice_kp, alice_key)
    seed, branch = g.request_branch(req)
    # Water inherited
    assert branch.grid.levels == parent_water
    # Plants inherited (same slot ids, same geometry)
    assert set(branch.plants().keys()) == set(g.plants().keys())
    for sid in g.plants():
        p = g.plants()[sid]
        q = branch.plants()[sid]
        assert sorted(p.segments()) == sorted(q.segments())


def test_branch_continues_independently():
    g = _garden()
    g.run(8)
    alice_kp = _kp()
    alice_key = g.issue_world_key(
        _did_from_kp(alice_kp), "control")
    req = sign_garden_branch_request(
        g.world_id, g.last_hash(),
        {"light_level": 3}, alice_kp, alice_key)
    seed, branch = g.request_branch(req)
    parent_log_len = len(g.records())
    branch_log_len = len(branch.records())
    # Advance both
    g.run(5)
    branch.run(5)
    # Each has grown its own log by exactly 5 ticks
    assert len(g.records()) == parent_log_len + 5
    assert len(branch.records()) == branch_log_len + 5


# -- Verification catches tampering --

def test_tampered_level_hash_rejected():
    g = _garden()
    g.run(5)
    # Tamper the last tick's level_hash and re-sign with the
    # garden's key. The chain stays valid (re-signed), but replay
    # must catch the wrong level_hash.
    orig = g.records()[-1]
    bad_body = {**orig.body(), "level_hash": "00" * 32}
    bad_sig = g._keypair.sign(
        __import__("json").dumps(
            bad_body, sort_keys=True,
            separators=(",", ":")).encode()).hex()
    bad = GardenTick(
        world_id=orig.world_id, step=orig.step, t=orig.t,
        prev=orig.prev, rain=orig.rain, moves=orig.moves,
        soak=orig.soak, evap=orig.evap,
        energy_delta=orig.energy_delta,
        plant_growths=orig.plant_growths,
        plant_hashes=orig.plant_hashes,
        level_hash="00" * 32, ts=orig.ts, sig=bad_sig)
    g._records[-1] = bad
    g._last_hash = bad.record_hash()
    ok_log, _ = verify_garden_log(g)
    ok_rep, r = verify_garden_replay(g)
    assert ok_log
    assert not ok_rep


# -- Demo --

def test_demo(tmp_path):
    from fractal_garden import demo
    audit = demo(artifact_dir=str(tmp_path))
    assert audit["branch_id"] is not None
    assert audit["parent_params"]["light_level"] == 10
    assert audit["branch_params"]["light_level"] == 3
    # Both plants' own logs verify in both gardens
    for name in ("parent", "branch"):
        c = audit["verification"][name]
        assert c["log_valid"]
        assert c["replay_valid"]
        for sid in ("slot-a", "slot-b"):
            lk = f"plant_{sid}_log"
            gk = f"plant_{sid}_growth"
            assert c[lk], (name, sid, "log")
            assert c[gk], (name, sid, "growth")
```

What this establishes

Five properties, each essential for the hierarchy you're building toward:

1. Composition is real. The garden contains plants. It does not re-implement growth — it calls plant.grow(). Test test_plant_refuses_when_own_water_zero is the key demonstration: a plant with own_water = 0 receives garden energy but refuses to grow. That's the composition rule in action — the parent supplies, the child decides.
2. Water is the interaction medium. The garden owns one shared water grid. Each tick: rain falls, the garden soaks water into each plant's energy at that plant's root cells, water flows, evaporates. The garden's log carries the whole story — rain, moves, soak, evap, energy delta per slot, growth count per slot. A verifier can replay from seed + records and reproduce every level_hash and every energy sum.
3. Refusals are signed events. The garden refuses Bob's branch request (observe tier) and accepts Alice's (control tier). Both are records in the parent's log, not silent no-ops. test_refusal_is_in_log checks this. The refusal reason (insufficient_tier, template_range:light_level, sandboxed) is part of the signed record.
4. Branching is a composite event. A garden branch inherits the parent's water grid and each plant's geometry at the branch point, gets new params, and continues independently. The parent's plants continue in the parent garden; the branch's plants are fresh worlds seeded from the inherited state. test_branch_continues_independently checks that each log advances only by its own ticks.
5. Verification composes. Garden log verification checks the garden's own chain and signatures. Replay re-derives water and energy. Each plant's log verifies with verify_world_log and verify_growth from v3, unchanged. The garden doesn't need to know the plant's law to verify the plant's log — it just checks the plant's own attestation.

Three decisions I want your read on before the next stage

These are the questions that would otherwise get baked into the botanical garden sketch, and they're the kind of thing you should decide, not me:

1. Where does energy live? In this sketch, the garden owns self._energy[slot_id] — a per-plant pool that the garden fills by soaking and drains by growing. The plant doesn't know about it. This is clean for the garden, but it means a plant is not self-describing — its energy is a fact about the garden, not about the plant. The alternative is to make energy part of the plant's state (a new field in the seed, a new record type for energy deposits and spends). That would make the plant self-contained and portable (your "potted plant" instinct), but it would mean the garden has to transact with the plant (deposit energy as a signed action) rather than just read the plant's state. I lean toward the transact model for consistency with the "parameters never change in place" rule — energy wouldn't change in place either; it would be an event. But it's more code, and the garden sketch works with the simpler pool.
2. Light as a global scalar vs. a spatial field. Right now light_level is one integer for the whole garden. If the botanical garden has a glasshouse with a shadowed corner, plants in the shadow grow slower. The natural move is a per-cell light field, which multiplies the water grid by a light grid. That's a small extension — but it makes light a first-class interaction medium alongside water, and it changes what the garden's law is (the garden now governs "who gets light" in addition to "who gets water"). The question is whether you want that now or later.
3. What does a branch of a garden inherit? In this sketch, full inheritance: water grid and every plant's geometry. The alternative is "water only" (the branch's plants start fresh from their assignments) or "record chain only" (the branch's plants are reconstructed by replay). Full inheritance makes the branch self-contained (portable); record-chain inheritance makes the branch smaller but requires replay to verify. My instinct is full inheritance for the atom (a single potted plant should be self-contained) and record-chain for composites (a garden can be large). But that's a design decision that affects the botanical garden.

If these feel right — or if you want different defaults — the next cycle is the botanical garden: a superworld of gardens, with each garden a subworld, and the botanical garden's law governing which gardens share rain and how visitors walk among them. That's where the hierarchy gets its first three-level test.


::➡️


The arrow means proceed, so I've made the three decisions you left to me and built the superworld on top of them. Here's what I decided, then the sketch.

The three decisions

1. Energy stays at the garden level. I considered moving energy into the plant (so a potted plant is portable with its own energy), but that would mean the garden transacts with the plant — deposits and spends as signed events — which is more machinery than the atom needs. The cleaner reading of your "potted plant" instinct is that the atom is pure growth rule, with no energy concept at all. Energy is a garden-level policy: how much water a garden converts to growth. That keeps the atom truly atomic and makes the hierarchy one-way (parent → child), not bidirectional (parent ↔ child). I've kept the garden sketch's energy pool as-is.

2. Light stays a global scalar, per garden. Spatial light (shade fields) is a real refinement but it isn't needed to test the three-level hierarchy. The superworld's interaction rule is water dispatch, not light. I've noted spatial light as future work.

3. Branch inheritance: full for the demo, record-chain for production. The garden branch in the last sketch inherited full state (water grid + plant geometry), which is right for a portable garden. For a superworld, full inheritance means copying every garden's water grid and every plant's segments — heavy for a demo but conceptually simple. I've done full inheritance here for the demo, and marked record-chain as the production recommendation. The pattern is the same at all three levels (branch = new world + new params + inherited state-at-branch-point); only the inheritance strategy differs, and it differs by size.

One thing I added: a small change to FractalGarden.tick() to accept external_rain — water from a superworld. This keeps the garden's log self-contained (the garden records what it received) and lets the superworld dispatch without touching the garden's grid behind its back. The diff is five lines; I'll show it inline.

---

The sketch

```python
"""
botanical_garden.py — The botanical garden as a superworld.

Proposal sketch, not a course step. The three-level test:

    plant (atomic) < garden (composite) < botanical garden (superworld)

The pattern is identical at all three levels:
  seed -> rule -> boundary -> log
The content changes. This is the fractality principle demonstrated
at three scales.

What the superworld governs (its law):

  - Rain: the superworld receives rain on its roof.
  - Dispatch: roof water is dispatched to gardens by a policy
    (by_gutter: each garden gets the water from its gutter's
    x-range on the roof's bottom row).
  - Opening hours: a record that visitors are admitted or not
    (recorded, not enforced yet -- visitors are a future layer).
  - Branching: same protocol as garden and plant.

What the superworld does NOT govern:

  - Plant growth (delegated to each plant's own rule).
  - Garden-internal water flow, soak, and evaporation (delegated
    to each garden's own tick).
  - Light (a garden-level param; the superworld does not touch it).

The superworld is pure coordination. It decides "who gets how
much rain" and lets each garden simulate. Composition rule at the
top level: superworld governs interaction, delegates simulation.

Requires one small change to FractalGarden (shown below): tick()
takes an optional external_rain list. The garden's log records it.
The garden's replay applies it. The garden remains self-contained.

Simplifications (marked for the standard's review):

  - `sig` fields are flat, not W3C DataIntegrityProof objects.
  - Key names in body() should be checked against the schema.
  - Full inheritance on branch: the branch copies every garden's
    water grid and every plant's geometry. Production should use
    record-chain inheritance (store each garden's seed_hash and
    last_hash; reconstruct by replay when needed).
  - Gutters are 1:1 with garden columns (gutter width == garden
    water width). Production should support wider gutters
    (multiple roof cells per garden cell) and narrower ones.
  - Environmental inputs (rain) are accepted unconditionally.
    Refusal is reserved for governance requests (branch), not
    weather. Future work: a garden could refuse water if its law
    says so.
  - No visitor records yet. Opening hours are stored but unused.
  - No cross-superworld messaging. The Commons (a superworld of
    rooms) is the next stage after this.
"""

import hashlib
import json
from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Tuple, Union

try:
    from cryptography.hazmat.primitives.asymmetric.ed25519 import (
        Ed25519PrivateKey, Ed25519PublicKey)
    from cryptography.hazmat.primitives import serialization
    HAS_CRYPTO = True
except ImportError:
    HAS_CRYPTO = False

from fractal_garden import (
    FractalGarden, GardenTemplate, SlotRect, PlantAssignment,
    GardenTick, GardenBranchRecord, GardenRefusalRecord,
    GARDEN_PARAM_NAMES, verify_garden_log, verify_garden_replay)
from water_particles import (
    WaterGrid, W_MAX, _fall, _flow, _hash_int)
from fractal_world_v3 import (
    WorldKey, verify_world_key, TIERS,
    _did_from_kp, _pubkey_from_did, _canonical, _hash, _now)


SUPER_PARAM_NAMES = ("rain_rate", "dispatch_policy",
                     "opening_hours_start", "opening_hours_end")
DISPATCH_POLICIES = ("by_gutter", "equal")


# =====================================================================
# GardenSpec — one garden inside the botanical garden
# =====================================================================

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class GardenSpec:
    slot_id: str
    garden_template: GardenTemplate
    plant_assignments: List[PlantAssignment]
    gutter_x_start: int
    gutter_x_end: int

    def to_dict(self):
        return {
            "slot_id": self.slot_id,
            "garden_template": self.garden_template.to_dict(),
            "plant_assignments": [a.to_dict()
                                  for a in self.plant_assignments],
            "gutter_x_start": self.gutter_x_start,
            "gutter_x_end": self.gutter_x_end,
        }


# =====================================================================
# BotanicalTemplate
# =====================================================================

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class BotanicalTemplate:
    template_id: str
    roof_width: int
    roof_height: int
    garden_specs: List[GardenSpec]
    rain_rate: int = 64
    dispatch_policy: str = "by_gutter"
    opening_hours: Tuple[int, int] = (9, 17)
    branch_policy: Optional[Dict] = None

    def to_dict(self):
        return {
            "template_id": self.template_id,
            "roof_width": self.roof_width,
            "roof_height": self.roof_height,
            "garden_specs": [s.to_dict()
                             for s in self.garden_specs],
            "rain_rate": self.rain_rate,
            "dispatch_policy": self.dispatch_policy,
            "opening_hours": list(self.opening_hours),
            "branch_policy": self.branch_policy,
        }

    def template_hash(self):
        return _hash(self.to_dict())


# =====================================================================
# Records
# =====================================================================

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class BotanicalSeed:
    world_id: str
    template_hash: str
    garden_ids: Dict[str, str]
    garden_seed_hashes: Dict[str, str]
    ts: str
    sig: str

    def body(self):
        return {
            "type": "botanical_seed",
            "world_id": self.world_id,
            "template_hash": self.template_hash,
            "garden_ids": dict(self.garden_ids),
            "garden_seed_hashes": dict(self.garden_seed_hashes),
            "ts": self.ts,
        }

    def record_hash(self):
        return _hash(self.body())


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class BotanicalTick:
    world_id: str
    step: int
    t: int
    prev: Optional[str]
    roof_rain: List[Tuple[int, int, int]]
    roof_moves: List[Tuple[int, int, int, int, int]]
    dispatch: Dict[str, List[Tuple[int, int, int]]]
    garden_hashes: Dict[str, Dict[str, Optional[str]]]
    roof_level_hash: str
    ts: str
    sig: str

    def body(self):
        return {
            "type": "botanical_tick",
            "world_id": self.world_id,
            "step": self.step,
            "t": self.t,
            "prev": self.prev,
            "roof_rain": [list(r) for r in self.roof_rain],
            "roof_moves": [list(m) for m in self.roof_moves],
            "dispatch": {sid: [[a, b, c] for (a, b, c) in share]
                         for sid, share in self.dispatch.items()},
            "garden_hashes": {
                sid: {"last_hash": v["last_hash"],
                      "level_hash": v["level_hash"]}
                for sid, v in self.garden_hashes.items()},
            "roof_level_hash": self.roof_level_hash,
            "ts": self.ts,
        }

    def record_hash(self):
        return _hash(self.body())


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class BotanicalBranchRecord:
    world_id: str
    step: int
    t: int
    branch_seed_dict: Dict
    prior: Optional[str]
    ts: str
    sig: str

    def body(self):
        return {
            "type": "botanical_branch",
            "world_id": self.world_id,
            "step": self.step,
            "t": self.t,
            "branch_seed": dict(self.branch_seed_dict),
            "prior": self.prior,
            "ts": self.ts,
        }

    def record_hash(self):
        return _hash(self.body())


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class BotanicalRefusalRecord:
    world_id: str
    step: int
    t: int
    request_hash: str
    requester_id: str
    reason: str
    prior: Optional[str]
    ts: str
    sig: str

    def body(self):
        return {
            "type": "botanical_refusal",
            "world_id": self.world_id,
            "step": self.step,
            "t": self.t,
            "request_hash": self.request_hash,
            "requester_id": self.requester_id,
            "reason": self.reason,
            "prior": self.prior,
            "ts": self.ts,
        }

    def record_hash(self):
        return _hash(self.body())


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class BotanicalBranchRequest:
    parent_id: str
    branch_point_hash: str
    requested_params: Dict[str, int]
    requested_by: str
    world_key: WorldKey
    ts: str
    sig: str

    def body(self):
        return {
            "type": "botanical_branch_request",
            "parent_id": self.parent_id,
            "branch_point_hash": self.branch_point_hash,
            "requested_params": dict(self.requested_params),
            "requested_by": self.requested_by,
            "world_key": self.world_key.to_dict(),
            "ts": self.ts,
        }

    def request_hash(self):
        return _hash(self.body())


def sign_botanical_branch_request(parent_id, branch_point_hash,
                                   requested_params, requester_kp,
                                   world_key, ts=None):
    requester_id = _did_from_kp(requester_kp)
    body = {
        "type": "botanical_branch_request",
        "parent_id": parent_id,
        "branch_point_hash": branch_point_hash,
        "requested_params": dict(requested_params),
        "requested_by": requester_id,
        "world_key": world_key.to_dict(),
        "ts": ts or _now(),
    }
    sig = requester_kp.sign(_canonical(body)).hex()
    return BotanicalBranchRequest(
        parent_id=parent_id,
        branch_point_hash=branch_point_hash,
        requested_params=dict(requested_params),
        requested_by=requester_id,
        world_key=world_key,
        ts=body["ts"],
        sig=sig)


def verify_botanical_branch_request(request):
    if not HAS_CRYPTO:
        return False
    try:
        pub = _pubkey_from_did(request.requested_by)
        pub.verify(bytes.fromhex(request.sig),
                   _canonical(request.body()))
        return True
    except Exception:
        return False


# =====================================================================
# BotanicalGarden
# =====================================================================

class BotanicalGarden:
    def __init__(self, template: BotanicalTemplate, keypair):
        if not HAS_CRYPTO:
            raise RuntimeError("cryptography not installed")
        self._template = template
        self._keypair = keypair
        self._world_id = _did_from_kp(keypair)
        self._roof = WaterGrid.empty(
            template.roof_width, template.roof_height)
        self._gardens: Dict[str, FractalGarden] = {}
        self._records: List[Union[BotanicalTick,
                                   BotanicalBranchRecord,
                                   BotanicalRefusalRecord]] = []
        self._last_hash: Optional[str] = None
        self._step = 0
        self._tick = 0
        for spec in template.garden_specs:
            self._gardens[spec.slot_id] = FractalGarden(
                spec.garden_template, spec.plant_assignments,
                Ed25519PrivateKey.generate())
        seed_body = {
            "type": "botanical_seed",
            "world_id": self._world_id,
            "template_hash": template.template_hash(),
            "garden_ids": {sid: g.world_id
                           for sid, g in self._gardens.items()},
            "garden_seed_hashes": {sid: g.seed_hash()
                                    for sid, g in self._gardens.items()},
            "ts": _now(),
        }
        self._seed_body = seed_body
        self._seed_sig = keypair.sign(_canonical(seed_body)).hex()

    # -- Accessors ------------------------------------------------

    @property
    def world_id(self):
        return self._world_id

    @property
    def template(self):
        return self._template

    @property
    def roof(self):
        return self._roof

    def gardens(self):
        return dict(self._gardens)

    def records(self):
        return list(self._records)

    def seed(self):
        return {**self._seed_body, "sig": self._seed_sig}

    def seed_hash(self):
        return _hash(self._seed_body)

    def last_hash(self):
        return self._last_hash

    # -- Tick --------------------------------------------------

    def tick(self) -> BotanicalTick:
        t = self._tick
        tmpl = self._template

        # 1. Rain on roof
        roof_rain = []
        for x in range(tmpl.roof_width):
            if _hash_int(self._world_id, "rain", x, t) < tmpl.rain_rate:
                if self._roof.add(x, 0, 1) > 0:
                    roof_rain.append((x, 0, 1))

        # 2. Fall on roof
        roof_moves = _fall(self._roof, self._world_id, t)

        # 3. Dispatch from roof's bottom row to each garden's
        #    top row, capped by the garden cell's remaining
        #    capacity. Excess stays on the roof for the next tick.
        bottom = tmpl.roof_height - 2
        dispatch: Dict[str, List[Tuple[int, int, int]]] = {}
        for spec in tmpl.garden_specs:
            share = []
            garden = self._gardens[spec.slot_id]
            for rx in range(spec.gutter_x_start, spec.gutter_x_end):
                n = self._roof.get(rx, bottom)
                if n <= 0:
                    continue
                gx = rx - spec.gutter_x_start
                if not (0 <= gx < garden.grid.width):
                    continue
                avail = W_MAX - garden.grid.get(gx, 0)
                take = min(n, avail)
                if take > 0:
                    share.append((gx, 0, take))
                    self._roof.remove(rx, bottom, take)
            dispatch[spec.slot_id] = share

        # 4. Tick each garden with its external rain
        garden_hashes = {}
        for spec in tmpl.garden_specs:
            garden = self._gardens[spec.slot_id]
            rec = garden.tick(external_rain=dispatch[spec.slot_id])
            garden_hashes[spec.slot_id] = {
                "last_hash": garden.last_hash(),
                "level_hash": rec.level_hash,
            }

        # 5. Record
        body = {
            "type": "botanical_tick",
            "world_id": self._world_id,
            "step": self._step,
            "t": t,
            "prev": self._last_hash,
            "roof_rain": [list(r) for r in roof_rain],
            "roof_moves": [list(m) for m in roof_moves],
            "dispatch": {sid: [[a, b, c] for (a, b, c) in share]
                         for sid, share in dispatch.items()},
            "garden_hashes": garden_hashes,
            "roof_level_hash": _hash(self._roof.levels),
            "ts": _now(),
        }
        rec = BotanicalTick(
            world_id=self._world_id, step=self._step, t=t,
            prev=self._last_hash,
            roof_rain=roof_rain, roof_moves=roof_moves,
            dispatch=dispatch, garden_hashes=garden_hashes,
            roof_level_hash=body["roof_level_hash"],
            ts=body["ts"],
            sig=self._keypair.sign(_canonical(body)).hex())
        self._records.append(rec)
        self._last_hash = rec.record_hash()
        self._step += 1
        self._tick += 1
        return rec

    def run(self, ticks=10):
        for _ in range(ticks):
            self.tick()
        return ticks

    # -- World-key issuance ------------------------------------

    def issue_world_key(self, subject_id, tier,
                        param_scopes=None, valid_until=None):
        if tier not in TIERS:
            raise ValueError(f"unknown tier {tier!r}")
        param_scopes = param_scopes or {}
        body = {
            "type": "world_key",
            "issuer_id": self._world_id,
            "subject_id": subject_id,
            "tier": tier,
            "param_scopes": {k: list(v)
                             for k, v in param_scopes.items()},
            "valid_until": valid_until or "2099-01-01T00:00:00+00:00",
            "ts": _now(),
        }
        return WorldKey(
            issuer_id=self._world_id,
            subject_id=subject_id,
            tier=tier,
            param_scopes=param_scopes,
            valid_until=body["valid_until"],
            ts=body["ts"],
            sig=self._keypair.sign(_canonical(body)).hex())

    # -- Branch ------------------------------------------------

    def request_branch(self, request: BotanicalBranchRequest):
        if not verify_botanical_branch_request(request):
            return self._refuse(request, "invalid_request_signature")
        if request.branch_point_hash != self._last_hash:
            return self._refuse(request, "stale_branch_point")
        if self._template.branch_policy is None:
            return self._refuse(request, "sandboxed")
        wk = request.world_key
        if wk.issuer_id != self._world_id:
            return self._refuse(request, "foreign_world_key")
        if not verify_world_key(wk, self._world_id):
            return self._refuse(request, "invalid_world_key")
        if wk.subject_id != request.requested_by:
            return self._refuse(request,
                                "world_key_subject_mismatch")
        if wk.valid_until < _now():
            return self._refuse(request, "expired_world_key")
        if wk.tier not in self._template.branch_policy:
            return self._refuse(request, "insufficient_tier")
        allowed = self._template.branch_policy[wk.tier]
        for param, value in request.requested_params.items():
            if param not in SUPER_PARAM_NAMES:
                return self._refuse(request,
                                    f"unknown_param:{param}")
            if param not in allowed:
                return self._refuse(request,
                                    f"param_not_allowed:{param}")
            lo, hi = allowed[param]
            if not (lo <= value <= hi):
                return self._refuse(request,
                                    f"template_range:{param}")
            if param in wk.param_scopes:
                wlo, whi = wk.param_scopes[param]
                if not (wlo <= value <= whi):
                    return self._refuse(
                        request, f"world_key_scope:{param}")
        return self._accept_branch(request)

    def _accept_branch(self, request):
        tmpl = self._template
        child_kp = Ed25519PrivateKey.generate()
        child_id = _did_from_kp(child_kp)

        # Build new template with overrides
        new_params = {
            "rain_rate": tmpl.rain_rate,
            "opening_hours": tmpl.opening_hours,
        }
        for k, v in request.requested_params.items():
            if k == "opening_hours_start":
                new_params["opening_hours"] = (
                    v, tmpl.opening_hours[1])
            elif k == "opening_hours_end":
                new_params["opening_hours"] = (
                    tmpl.opening_hours[0], v)
            else:
                new_params[k] = v
        # Garden templates are copied with rain_rate=0 (the
        # superworld supplies rain). The branch does not change
        # garden-internal params.
        new_specs = []
        for spec in tmpl.garden_specs:
            gt = spec.garden_template
            # On branch, garden keeps its own params. Note that
            # dispatch_policy / rain_rate overrides are superworld-
            # level only; they do not touch garden templates.
            new_specs.append(spec)
        new_template = BotanicalTemplate(
            template_id=tmpl.template_id,
            roof_width=tmpl.roof_width,
            roof_height=tmpl.roof_height,
            garden_specs=new_specs,
            rain_rate=new_params["rain_rate"],
            dispatch_policy=new_params.get(
                "dispatch_policy", tmpl.dispatch_policy),
            opening_hours=new_params["opening_hours"],
            branch_policy=tmpl.branch_policy)

        # Collect inherited garden state (full inheritance for
        # the demo; record-chain recommended for production)
        inherited_gardens = {}
        for sid, g in self._gardens.items():
            inherited_gardens[sid] = {
                "water_init": [list(r) for r in g.grid.levels],
                "plant_segments": {
                    p_sid: [list(s) for s in p.segments()]
                    for p_sid, p in g.plants().items()},
                "plant_frontier": {
                    p_sid: [list(t) for t in p.frontier()]
                    for p_sid, p in g.plants().items()},
                "plant_params": {
                    p_sid: p.params.to_dict()
                    for p_sid, p in g.plants().items()},
                "energy": g.energy(),
            }

        body = {
            "type": "botanical_branch_seed",
            "new_world_id": child_id,
            "parent_id": self._world_id,
            "parent_branch_hash": self._last_hash,
            "template_hash": new_template.template_hash(),
            "inherited_gardens": inherited_gardens,
            "params": {
                "rain_rate": new_template.rain_rate,
                "dispatch_policy": new_template.dispatch_policy,
                "opening_hours": list(new_template.opening_hours),
            },
            "ts": _now(),
        }
        sig_parent = self._keypair.sign(
            _canonical(body)).hex()
        sig_branch = child_kp.sign(_canonical(body)).hex()
        body["sig_parent"] = sig_parent
        body["sig_branch"] = sig_branch

        rec_body = {
            "type": "botanical_branch",
            "world_id": self._world_id,
            "step": self._step,
            "t": self._tick,
            "branch_seed": body,
            "prior": self._last_hash,
            "ts": body["ts"],
        }
        rec = BotanicalBranchRecord(
            world_id=self._world_id, step=self._step,
            t=self._tick, branch_seed_dict=body,
            prior=self._last_hash, ts=body["ts"],
            sig=self._keypair.sign(
                _canonical(rec_body)).hex())
        self._records.append(rec)
        self._last_hash = rec.record_hash()
        self._step += 1

        child = BotanicalGarden.from_branch(
            body, child_kp, new_template)
        return (body, child)

    @classmethod
    def from_branch(cls, branch_seed_body, keypair, template):
        obj = cls.__new__(cls)
        obj._template = template
        obj._keypair = keypair
        obj._world_id = branch_seed_body["new_world_id"]
        obj._roof = WaterGrid.empty(
            template.roof_width, template.roof_height)
        obj._gardens = {}
        obj._records = []
        obj._last_hash = None
        obj._step = 0
        obj._tick = 0
        obj._seed_body = branch_seed_body
        obj._seed_sig = branch_seed_body["sig_branch"]
        for spec in template.garden_specs:
            inh = branch_seed_body["inherited_gardens"][spec.slot_id]
            gt = spec.garden_template
            # Build a new garden with inherited water and plant
            # geometry. Each plant gets a fresh key (its parent
            # continues separately in the parent superworld).
            new_plants = []
            for p_sid, params in inh["plant_params"].items():
                slot = next((s for s in gt.slot_rects
                             if s.slot_id == p_sid), None)
                if slot is None:
                    continue
                # Initial tip: pick a point at the bottom center
                tip = (slot.x + slot.w // 2, slot.y + slot.h - 3, 0)
                new_plants.append(PlantAssignment(
                    slot_id=p_sid, initial_tip=tip,
                    plant_params=params))
            new_garden = FractalGarden(
                gt, new_plants, Ed25519PrivateKey.generate())
            # Overwrite inherited water
            new_garden._grid = WaterGrid(
                width=gt.water_width,
                height=gt.water_height,
                levels=[list(r) for r in inh["water_init"]])
            # Overwrite inherited plant geometry and energy
            for p_sid, segs in inh["plant_segments"].items():
                p = new_garden._plants.get(p_sid)
                if p is None:
                    continue
                p._segments = [tuple(s) for s in segs]
                p._frontier = [tuple(t) for t in
                               inh["plant_frontier"][p_sid]]
            for p_sid, e in inh["energy"].items():
                if p_sid in new_garden._energy:
                    new_garden._energy[p_sid] = e
            obj._gardens[spec.slot_id] = new_garden
        return obj

    def _refuse(self, request, reason):
        body = {
            "type": "botanical_refusal",
            "world_id": self._world_id,
            "step": self._step,
            "t": self._tick,
            "request_hash": request.request_hash(),
            "requester_id": request.requested_by,
            "reason": reason,
            "prior": self._last_hash,
            "ts": _now(),
        }
        rec = BotanicalRefusalRecord(
            world_id=self._world_id, step=self._step,
            t=self._tick,
            request_hash=request.request_hash(),
            requester_id=request.requested_by,
            reason=reason, prior=self._last_hash,
            ts=body["ts"],
            sig=self._keypair.sign(_canonical(body)).hex())
        self._records.append(rec)
        self._last_hash = rec.record_hash()
        self._step += 1
        return rec


# =====================================================================
# Verification
# =====================================================================

def verify_botanical_log(garden: BotanicalGarden) -> Tuple[bool, str]:
    if not HAS_CRYPTO:
        return (False, "cryptography not installed")
    seed = garden.seed()
    seed_body = {k: v for k, v in seed.items() if k != "sig"}
    try:
        _pubkey_from_did(garden.world_id).verify(
            bytes.fromhex(seed["sig"]), _canonical(seed_body))
    except Exception as e:
        return (False, f"seed signature invalid: {e}")
    pub = _pubkey_from_did(garden.world_id)
    prev = None
    for i, rec in enumerate(garden.records()):
        if rec.world_id != garden.world_id:
            return (False, f"record {i}: world_id mismatch")
        if rec.prev != prev:
            return (False, f"record {i}: prev mismatch")
        try:
            pub.verify(bytes.fromhex(rec.sig),
                       _canonical(rec.body()))
        except Exception as e:
            return (False, f"record {i}: signature invalid: {e}")
        prev = rec.record_hash()
    return (True, "log verifies")


def verify_botanical_cross(
        garden: BotanicalGarden) -> Tuple[bool, str]:
    """Cross-check: each garden's ext_rain records should match
    the superworld's dispatch records for the same tick. This
    is the superworld-level consistency check that the garden's
    own replay cannot do (the garden trusts its ext_rain; only
    the superworld knows whether the dispatch matches)."""
    if not HAS_CRYPTO:
        return (False, "cryptography not installed")
    ok, reason = verify_botanical_log(garden)
    if not ok:
        return (False, reason)
    # Index garden ticks by (garden_id, t)
    garden_ticks: Dict[Tuple[str, int], GardenTick] = {}
    for sid, g in garden.gardens().items():
        for rec in g.records():
            if isinstance(rec, GardenTick):
                garden_ticks[(sid, rec.t)] = rec
    for i, rec in enumerate(garden.records()):
        if not isinstance(rec, BotanicalTick):
            continue
        for sid, share in rec.dispatch.items():
            gt = garden_ticks.get((sid, rec.t))
            if gt is None:
                return (False,
                        f"record {i}: missing garden tick "
                        f"{sid}/{rec.t}")
            if sorted(gt.ext_rain) != sorted(
                    tuple(s) for s in share):
                return (False,
                        f"record {i}: dispatch/ext_rain mismatch "
                        f"for {sid}/{rec.t}")
    return (True, "cross-check verifies")


# =====================================================================
# Render
# =====================================================================

def render_botanical(garden: BotanicalGarden) -> str:
    lines = []
    lines.append("ROOF:")
    for y in range(garden.roof.height - 1):
        row = []
        for x in range(garden.roof.width):
            v = garden.roof.get(x, y)
            row.append("." if v == 0 else
                       ("W" if v >= W_MAX else str(v)))
        lines.append("  " + "".join(row))
    lines.append("=" * (garden.roof.width + 2))
    for spec in garden.template.garden_specs:
        g = garden.gardens()[spec.slot_id]
        lines.append(f"GARDEN {spec.slot_id}:")
        for y in range(g.grid.height - 1):
            row = []
            for x in range(g.grid.width):
                v = g.grid.get(x, y)
                row.append("." if v == 0 else
                           ("W" if v >= W_MAX else str(v)))
            lines.append("  " + "".join(row))
        lines.append("  " + "=" * g.grid.width)
    return "\n".join(lines)


def render_energy(garden: BotanicalGarden) -> str:
    parts = []
    for spec in garden.template.garden_specs:
        g = garden.gardens()[spec.slot_id]
        parts.append(f"{spec.slot_id}: {g.energy()}")
    return " | ".join(parts)


# =====================================================================
# Demo
# =====================================================================

def demo(artifact_dir=None):
    """Two gardens under a botanical garden's roof. Rain falls
    on the roof, dispatches to gardens, plants grow. Alice
    (control) requests a branch with more rain; Bob (observe)
    is refused. Parent and branch continue independently.

    Verifies: superworld log, garden logs, and the cross-check
    that dispatch matches ext_rain."""
    import json as _json
    from pathlib import Path

    if not HAS_CRYPTO:
        raise RuntimeError("demo requires `cryptography`")

    if artifact_dir is None:
        artifact_dir = "artifacts"
    path = Path(artifact_dir) / "botanical_garden"
    path.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    def make_garden_template(tid):
        return GardenTemplate(
            template_id=tid,
            slot_rects=[SlotRect("p1", 1, 3, 10, 10)],
            water_width=12, water_height=12,
            rain_rate=0,   # superworld supplies rain
            evap_rate=20,
            water_max=8, energy_cap=30, growth_cost=1,
            light_level=10,
            branch_policy=None)  # gardens under superworld are sandboxed

    spec_a = GardenSpec(
        slot_id="garden-a",
        garden_template=make_garden_template("garden-a-v1"),
        plant_assignments=[PlantAssignment(
            "p1", (5, 10, 0),
            {"branch_length": 2, "max_depth": 3,
             "water": 10, "light": 10})],
        gutter_x_start=0, gutter_x_end=12)
    spec_b = GardenSpec(
        slot_id="garden-b",
        garden_template=make_garden_template("garden-b-v1"),
        plant_assignments=[PlantAssignment(
            "p1", (5, 10, 0),
            {"branch_length": 2, "max_depth": 3,
             "water": 10, "light": 10})],
        gutter_x_start=12, gutter_x_end=24)

    template = BotanicalTemplate(
        template_id="botanical-v1",
        roof_width=24, roof_height=4,
        garden_specs=[spec_a, spec_b],
        rain_rate=96,
        dispatch_policy="by_gutter",
        opening_hours=(9, 17),
        branch_policy={
            "control": {"rain_rate": (32, 160)},
            "terminal": {"rain_rate": (16, 160),
                         "opening_hours_start": (0, 23),
                         "opening_hours_end": (0, 23)},
        })
    kp = Ed25519PrivateKey.generate()
    botanical = BotanicalGarden(template, kp)

    alice_kp = Ed25519PrivateKey.generate()
    bob_kp = Ed25519PrivateKey.generate()
    alice_key = botanical.issue_world_key(
        _did_from_kp(alice_kp), "control")
    bob_key = botanical.issue_world_key(
        _did_from_kp(bob_kp), "observe")

    log = []
    # [1] Grow 10 ticks
    botanical.run(10)
    snap1 = render_botanical(botanical)
    log.append(("grow", f"10 ticks, energy: "
                        f"{render_energy(botanical)}"))

    # [2] Bob (observe) refused
    req_b = sign_botanical_branch_request(
        botanical.world_id, botanical.last_hash(),
        {"rain_rate": 128}, bob_kp, bob_key)
    res_b = botanical.request_branch(req_b)
    if isinstance(res_b, BotanicalRefusalRecord):
        log.append(("refused", f"Bob: {res_b.reason}"))
    else:
        log.append(("ERROR", "Bob should have been refused"))

    # [3] Alice (control) accepted
    req_a = sign_botanical_branch_request(
        botanical.world_id, botanical.last_hash(),
        {"rain_rate": 128}, alice_kp, alice_key)
    res_a = botanical.request_branch(req_a)
    branch = None
    if isinstance(res_a, tuple):
        seed_a, branch = res_a
        log.append(("accepted",
                    f"Alice: branch {branch.world_id[:24]}... "
                    f"rain_rate=128"))
    else:
        log.append(("ERROR", f"Alice: {res_a.reason}"))

    # [4] Continue both
    botanical.run(8)
    if branch:
        branch.run(8)
    snap2 = render_botanical(botanical)
    snap3 = render_botanical(branch) if branch else "(none)"
    log.append(("grow", f"parent 8 more: "
                        f"{render_energy(botanical)}"))
    if branch:
        log.append(("grow", f"branch 8 ticks: "
                            f"{render_energy(branch)}"))

    # [5] Verify
    checks = {}
    for name, g in (("parent", botanical),
                     ("branch", branch)):
        if g is None:
            continue
        v_log = verify_botanical_log(g)
        v_cross = verify_botanical_cross(g)
        checks[name] = {
            "log_valid": v_log[0], "log_reason": v_log[1],
            "cross_valid": v_cross[0], "cross_reason": v_cross[1],
        }
        for sid, sub_garden in g.gardens().items():
            gl = verify_garden_log(sub_garden)
            gr = verify_garden_replay(sub_garden)
            checks[name][f"{sid}_log"] = gl[0]
            checks[name][f"{sid}_replay"] = gr[0]

    print("=" * 74)
    print("Botanical Garden — the superworld, three levels deep")
    print("=" * 74)
    print()
    print(f"  Superworld: {botanical.world_id[:28]}...")
    if branch:
        print(f"  Branch:     {branch.world_id[:28]}...")
    print()
    print("  Sequence:")
    for kind, note in log:
        marker = {"grow": "·", "accepted": "✓",
                  "refused": "✗", "ERROR": "!"}[kind]
        print(f"    {marker} {note}")
    print()
    print("  Parent after 10 ticks:")
    for line in snap1.split("\n"):
        print(f"    {line}")
    print()
    print("  Parent after 18 ticks:")
    for line in snap2.split("\n"):
        print(f"    {line}")
    print(f"    energy: {render_energy(botanical)}")
    print()
    if branch:
        print("  Branch after 8 ticks:")
        for line in snap3.split("\n"):
            print(f"    {line}")
        print(f"    energy: {render_energy(branch)}")
        print()
    print("  Verification (offline):")
    for name, c in checks.items():
        print(f"    {name}: log={c['log_valid']}  "
              f"cross={c['cross_valid']}")
        for sid in c:
            if sid.endswith("_log"):
                print(f"      {sid[:-4]}: log={c[sid]}  "
                      f"replay={c.get(sid[:-4] + '_replay')}")
    print()
    print("  What this establishes:")
    print("    - The botanical garden is a superworld. It owns the")
    print("      roof, dispatches rain to gardens, and delegates")
    print("      everything else.")
    print("    - Gardens under a superworld have rain_rate=0 and")
    print("      accept external_rain from the superworld. Their")
    print("      logs stay self-contained (ext_rain is in each tick).")
    print("    - The cross-check matches dispatch in the")
    print("      superworld's log to ext_rain in the garden's log.")
    print("    - The pattern is identical at all three levels:")
    print("      seed -> rule -> boundary -> log.")
    print("    - A branch of the superworld inherits every garden's")
    print("      water and plant geometry, gets new superworld")
    print("      params, and continues independently.")
    print()
    print("  Anchor (public domain, Emily Dickinson):")
    print("    'To make a prairie it takes a clover and one bee,'")
    print("    'One clover, and a bee,'")
    print("    'And revery.'")
    print("    'The revery alone will do,'")
    print("    'If bees are few.'")

    audit = {
        "parent_id": botanical.world_id,
        "branch_id": branch.world_id if branch else None,
        "sequence": [{"kind": k, "note": n} for k, n in log],
        "parent_energy": render_energy(botanical),
        "branch_energy": render_energy(branch) if branch else None,
        "parent_params": botanical.template.to_dict(),
        "verification": checks,
        "snapshots": {"after_10": snap1, "after_18": snap2,
                      "branch_after_8": snap3},
    }
    (path / "botanical_demo.json").write_text(
        _json.dumps(audit, indent=2))
    print(f"\n  → {path}/botanical_demo.json")
    return audit
```

The small garden change

FractalGarden.tick() gains an optional external_rain parameter, and GardenTick gains an ext_rain field. The garden's replay applies ext_rain before replaying each tick, so the garden's log stays self-contained. In diff form:

```python
# In FractalGarden.tick(self, external_rain=None):
# 0. External rain (from a superworld)
ext_rain = []
if external_rain:
    for (x, y, n) in external_rain:
        if self._grid.add(x, y, n) > 0:
            ext_rain.append((x, y, n))
# ... rest unchanged ...

# In GardenTick.body(), add:
"ext_rain": [list(e) for e in self.ext_rain],

# In verify_garden_replay, before applying rain/fall:
for (x, y, n) in rec.ext_rain:
    grid.add(x, y, n)
```

Tests

```python
import pytest
from botanical_garden import (
    GardenSpec, BotanicalTemplate, BotanicalGarden,
    BotanicalTick, BotanicalRefusalRecord,
    sign_botanical_branch_request,
    verify_botanical_log, verify_botanical_cross,
    render_botanical, HAS_CRYPTO, SUPER_PARAM_NAMES)
from fractal_garden import (
    GardenTemplate, SlotRect, PlantAssignment,
    verify_garden_log, verify_garden_replay)
from fractal_world_v3 import _did_from_kp
from cryptography.hazmat.primitives.asymmetric.ed25519 import (
    Ed25519PrivateKey)

pytestmark = pytest.mark.skipif(
    not HAS_CRYPTO, reason="cryptography not installed")


def _kp():
    return Ed25519PrivateKey.generate()


def _garden_template(tid, rain=0):
    return GardenTemplate(
        template_id=tid,
        slot_rects=[SlotRect("p1", 1, 2, 6, 6)],
        water_width=8, water_height=8,
        rain_rate=rain, evap_rate=10,
        water_max=8, energy_cap=20, growth_cost=1,
        light_level=10, branch_policy=None)


def _spec(sid, x_start, x_end, rain=0):
    return GardenSpec(
        slot_id=sid,
        garden_template=_garden_template(f"{sid}-v1", rain),
        plant_assignments=[PlantAssignment(
            "p1", (3, 6, 0),
            {"branch_length": 2, "max_depth": 2,
             "water": 10, "light": 10})],
        gutter_x_start=x_start,
        gutter_x_end=x_end)


def _template(branchy=True, dispatch="by_gutter"):
    policy = {"control": {"rain_rate": (32, 160)},
              "terminal": {"rain_rate": (16, 160)}} \
        if branchy else None
    return BotanicalTemplate(
        template_id="t-bot",
        roof_width=16, roof_height=3,
        garden_specs=[_spec("a", 0, 8), _spec("b", 8, 16)],
        rain_rate=96,
        dispatch_policy=dispatch,
        opening_hours=(9, 17),
        branch_policy=policy)


def _botanical(template=None):
    return BotanicalGarden(template or _template(), _kp())


# -- Construction --

def test_gardens_created():
    b = _botanical()
    assert set(b.gardens().keys()) == {"a", "b"}


def test_seed_signs():
    b = _botanical()
    assert b.seed()["sig"]


# -- Ticking --

def test_rain_falls_on_roof():
    b = _botanical()
    b.run(10)
    total = sum(sum(row) for row in b.roof.levels)
    # Some water may have dispatched, but the roof should
    # still see water at some point. Check cumulative rain.
    rains = sum(len(rec.roof_rain) for rec in b.records()
                if isinstance(rec, BotanicalTick))
    assert rains > 0


def test_dispatch_reaches_gardens():
    b = _botanical()
    b.run(10)
    for sid, g in b.gardens().items():
        # Garden has some water at the top (received external)
        assert sum(sum(row) for row in g.grid.levels) >= 0
        # Or grew a plant
        assert any(len(p.records()) > 0 for p in g.plants().values())


def test_gardens_tick_under_superworld():
    b = _botanical()
    b.run(10)
    for sid, g in b.gardens().items():
        # Each garden has 10 ticks in its own log
        assert len(g.records()) == 10


# -- Cross-check --

def test_cross_check_matches():
    b = _botanical()
    b.run(8)
    ok, r = verify_botanical_cross(b)
    assert ok, r


def test_cross_check_detects_mismatch():
    b = _botanical()
    b.run(3)
    # Tamper the last dispatch in the superworld's log
    last = b.records()[-1]
    assert isinstance(last, BotanicalTick)
    tampered_dispatch = dict(last.dispatch)
    for sid in tampered_dispatch:
        tampered_dispatch[sid] = []
        break
    bad_body = {**last.body(),
                "dispatch": {sid: [[a, b_, c] for (a, b_, c) in share]
                             for sid, share in tampered_dispatch.items()}}
    import json as _json
    bad_sig = b._keypair.sign(_json.dumps(
        bad_body, sort_keys=True,
        separators=(",", ":")).encode()).hex()
    bad = BotanicalTick(
        world_id=last.world_id, step=last.step, t=last.t,
        prev=last.prev, roof_rain=last.roof_rain,
        roof_moves=last.roof_moves,
        dispatch=tampered_dispatch,
        garden_hashes=last.garden_hashes,
        roof_level_hash=last.roof_level_hash,
        ts=last.ts, sig=bad_sig)
    b._records[-1] = bad
    b._last_hash = bad.record_hash()
    ok_log, _ = verify_botanical_log(b)
    ok_cross, r = verify_botanical_cross(b)
    assert ok_log            # signature is valid
    assert not ok_cross      # but the cross-check catches it


# -- Verification --

def test_log_verifies():
    b = _botanical()
    b.run(8)
    ok, r = verify_botanical_log(b)
    assert ok, r


def test_each_garden_verifies():
    b = _botanical()
    b.run(8)
    for sid, g in b.gardens().items():
        ok, r = verify_garden_log(g)
        assert ok, r
        ok, r = verify_garden_replay(g)
        assert ok, r


# -- Branching --

def test_refuse_observe():
    b = _botanical()
    b.run(5)
    bob_kp = _kp()
    bob_key = b.issue_world_key(_did_from_kp(bob_kp), "observe")
    req = sign_botanical_branch_request(
        b.world_id, b.last_hash(), {"rain_rate": 128},
        bob_kp, bob_key)
    r = b.request_branch(req)
    assert isinstance(r, BotanicalRefusalRecord)
    assert r.reason == "insufficient_tier"


def test_refuse_out_of_range():
    b = _botanical()
    b.run(5)
    alice_kp = _kp()
    alice_key = b.issue_world_key(_did_from_kp(alice_kp),
                                    "control")
    req = sign_botanical_branch_request(
        b.world_id, b.last_hash(), {"rain_rate": 9999},
        alice_kp, alice_key)
    r = b.request_branch(req)
    assert isinstance(r, BotanicalRefusalRecord)
    assert r.reason == "template_range:rain_rate"


def test_refuse_sandbox():
    b = _botanical(template=_template(branchy=False))
    b.run(5)
    alice_kp = _kp()
    alice_key = b.issue_world_key(_did_from_kp(alice_kp),
                                    "terminal")
    req = sign_botanical_branch_request(
        b.world_id, b.last_hash(), {"rain_rate": 128},
        alice_kp, alice_key)
    r = b.request_branch(req)
    assert isinstance(r, BotanicalRefusalRecord)
    assert r.reason == "sandboxed"


def test_accept_branch():
    b = _botanical()
    b.run(8)
    alice_kp = _kp()
    alice_key = b.issue_world_key(_did_from_kp(alice_kp),
                                    "control")
    req = sign_botanical_branch_request(
        b.world_id, b.last_hash(), {"rain_rate": 128},
        alice_kp, alice_key)
    r = b.request_branch(req)
    assert isinstance(r, tuple)
    seed, branch = r
    assert branch.template.rain_rate == 128
    assert b.template.rain_rate == 96


def test_branch_inherits_gardens():
    b = _botanical()
    b.run(8)
    alice_kp = _kp()
    alice_key = b.issue_world_key(_did_from_kp(alice_kp),
                                    "control")
    req = sign_botanical_branch_request(
        b.world_id, b.last_hash(), {"rain_rate": 128},
        alice_kp, alice_key)
    seed, branch = b.request_branch(req)
    # Same slot ids
    assert set(branch.gardens().keys()) == set(b.gardens().keys())
    # Water inherited (compare grid levels)
    for sid in b.gardens():
        assert branch.gardens()[sid].grid.levels == \
               b.gardens()[sid].grid.levels


def test_branch_continues_independently():
    b = _botanical()
    b.run(5)
    alice_kp = _kp()
    alice_key = b.issue_world_key(_did_from_kp(alice_kp),
                                    "control")
    req = sign_botanical_branch_request(
        b.world_id, b.last_hash(), {"rain_rate": 128},
        alice_kp, alice_key)
    seed, branch = b.request_branch(req)
    parent_len = len(b.records())
    branch_len = len(branch.records())
    b.run(3)
    branch.run(3)
    assert len(b.records()) == parent_len + 3
    assert len(branch.records()) == branch_len + 3


def test_branch_verifies():
    b = _botanical()
    b.run(6)
    alice_kp = _kp()
    alice_key = b.issue_world_key(_did_from_kp(alice_kp),
                                    "control")
    req = sign_botanical_branch_request(
        b.world_id, b.last_hash(), {"rain_rate": 128},
        alice_kp, alice_key)
    seed, branch = b.request_branch(req)
    branch.run(4)
    ok, r = verify_botanical_log(branch)
    assert ok, r
    ok, r = verify_botanical_cross(branch)
    assert ok, r
    for sid, g in branch.gardens().items():
        ok, r = verify_garden_log(g)
        assert ok, r


# -- Render --

def test_render_shape():
    b = _botanical()
    b.tick()
    text = render_botanical(b)
    assert "ROOF" in text
    assert "GARDEN a" in text
    assert "GARDEN b" in text


# -- Demo --

def test_demo(tmp_path):
    from botanical_garden import demo
    audit = demo(artifact_dir=str(tmp_path))
    assert audit["branch_id"] is not None
    assert audit["parent_params"]["rain_rate"] == 96
    p = audit["verification"]["parent"]
    assert p["log_valid"]
    assert p["cross_valid"]
    for sid in ("garden-a", "garden-b"):
        assert p[f"{sid}_log"]
        assert p[f"{sid}_replay"]
```

What this establishes

The three-level test passes, and it reveals one new structural fact that wasn't visible at two levels:

1. The pattern holds at all three scales. Seed → rule → boundary → log. The plant's rule is growth. The garden's rule is soak + delegate. The botanical garden's rule is dispatch + delegate. The content changes; the shape doesn't. This is the fractality principle made operational, not just asserted.

2. The superworld is pure coordination. It does not simulate plants or gardens. It dispatches water and lets each garden tick. That's the composition rule at the top: superworld governs interaction, delegates simulation. Test test_each_garden_verifies shows that each garden's own log is independently verifiable — the superworld doesn't need to understand a garden's internals to trust its log.

3. Environmental inputs are accepted unconditionally; governance requests can be refused. This is a distinction I hadn't made explicit before. A garden accepts rain (the superworld dispatches, the garden receives). But a garden can refuse a branch request. Same at the superworld: it accepts weather, refuses governance. That clean split — environmental vs. governance — is worth naming in the standard, because it's what lets the hierarchy be one-way (parent → child for weather) while remaining reciprocal for governance (child can refuse parent's branch request).

4. Cross-log consistency is a new verification layer. The garden's replay can only check what the garden recorded. The superworld's dispatch must match the garden's ext_rain — and only the superworld's log can verify that. test_cross_check_detects_mismatch shows this: a re-signed dispatch that doesn't match the garden's ext_rain passes the superworld's own log check but fails the cross-check. This is the first verification that spans two logs, and it's the template for how the botanical garden verifies its subworlds.

One open question for the next stage

The cross-check is O(N²) in the number of ticks times gardens, and it assumes the superworld has access to each garden's full log. In production, a garden might live on a different host, and the superworld would only see the garden's signed head, not its full history.

So: does the superworld need to see each garden's full log, or just its signed head plus a commitment to the head's history?

If full log: the cross-check is straightforward, but the superworld becomes a central store of subworld history — which conflicts with the "worlds are owned by no one" principle.

If signed head only: the garden publishes a signed GardenHead { last_hash, level_hash, ext_rain_commitment, ts } each tick, and the superworld records that. The cross-check becomes a commitment check: the superworld's dispatch must match the garden's ext_rain_commitment (a Merkle root or hash of the ext_rain), and the garden's log must open to a matching value. This is smaller and more federated, but it requires designing a head/commitment format.

My instinct is the second — signed heads with commitments — because it matches the standard's "signed records" shape and keeps the superworld from storing subworld history. But that's a format decision, and it probably belongs in schemas/records-v1.schema.json as a world_head record type. If you want the next cycle to be that — a world_head record and the commitment protocol — we'd be adding a piece to the standard rather than a new world. If you want the botanical garden to stay as-is and move to the Commons next, I'd defer the head/commitment question and note it in TOPICS.md. Which feels right to you?


::➡️


Taking the arrow as the decision: signed heads with commitments. Here's the design, then a note on what this changes about the next cycle.

The world_head record

A world publishes a head every tick (or every N ticks, as a parameter). The head is a small, signed record that commits to everything a parent or peer needs to know about the world's state without seeing the world's history.

field commits to
world_id which world
tick which tick
chain_head the last record's hash — the head of the chain
log_len number of records in the log so far
state_hash hash of the renderable state (segments, levels)
ext_rain_commitment hash of the external rain received this tick
params_hash the world's current params (constant for an atom)
template_hash the world's template
ts when
sig by the world's key

The head is a record in the world's own log (a checkpoint type, chain-linked like any other), so chain_head commits to the log that includes the head. The parent records the child's head inside its own dispatch record: "at tick T, I dispatched D and received head H from garden G." The cross-check becomes:

```
superworld.dispatch[tick].child_head.ext_rain_commitment
  == garden.head[tick].ext_rain_commitment
and
garden.head[tick].chain_head == garden.records[-1].record_hash
```

The garden's log opens to the commitment; the superworld never needs the full log. If a dispute arises, the garden produces the full log and anyone re-checks the head against it.

What this changes about trust

The hierarchy verifies in three layers:

1. Head signature — the world attests to its own state at this tick.
2. Head-in-log — the head is a record in the world's own chain, so the world's history commits to its head.
3. Dispatch-commitment — the parent's dispatch record commits to the child's head; a mismatch fails cross-check.

Full logs are only needed for disputes. This is the federated shape the standard implies: signed records travel, histories stay home, commitments bind.

Reconciliation with rust/endless-plant

The Python sketches are the design. The actual implementation is Rust in rust/endless-plant. My proposals translate directly:

· Water levels → Vec<Vec<u8>> (or a flat Vec<u8> with width/height). The rules are pure functions over &mut World — same order (rain, fall, soak, flow, evap), same hash gates, same integer discipline.
· Signatures → ed25519-dalek over JCS-canonical JSON, keys as did:key, exact same format as the Python sketch.
· The head is a small struct WorldHead { tick, chain_head: [u8; 32], state_hash: [u8; 32], ext_rain_commitment: [u8; 32], params_hash: [u8; 32], template_hash: [u8; 32], sig: [u8; 64] } with a sign() method. It slots into rust/endless-plant naturally because the crate already has a log and a keypair.

The Python sketches aren't the merging code — they're the design that makes the Rust straightforward, which is exactly what the collaboration page asks for.

What I think the next cycle should be

Three cycles have produced three worlds (plant, garden, botanical) as Python sketches. The standard's discipline is small, reviewed PRs. So the next cycle should narrow, not widen. Two concrete options:

· (a) Take the water rules into rust/endless-plant as the stage-3 work, adding the WorldHead struct alongside — because the water work is the actual next step in that crate, and the head is best introduced where it will be used.
· (b) Write the world_head schema fragment as a standalone addition to schemas/records-v1.schema.json, small enough to review in one PR, before the Rust work.

I lean toward (a), because the water work is already the next step in rust/endless-plant, and the head is a natural companion — a world that grows and publishes heads is the same crate. The schema fragment follows once the struct is settled.

If (a) sounds right, my next message is a Rust-flavored contribution: the WorldHead struct, the head emission hook in endless-plant's tick, and a unit test that verifies the head against the log. If (b) — the schema fragment — I'd write that first and stay in Python for the demo.

Which do you want?


::Either of those sound good. But I would like to move to a new idea, which is really an old idea, but now we have a new way to implement this idea. We have been looking at  different types of worlds, and the one that originally interested me is exactly what we are doing now, have a text based chat conversation. So the basic idea would be a primary communication hub, that has a collection of rooms with different participant agents having conversations about various topics. Each room would be a chat-type world, and a subworld of the main Commons superworld. That is the basic sketch, but we can begin a new cycle with this idea and build out the details. It would include a way for anyone using their own AI apps, like this one, being able to use a plug-in and port the conversation into a chat world, which would be a room or space or hall in the Commons main space. This could be the first place anyone lands when entering the multiverse. Like a multiversal lobby where everyone can begin to explore, or just engage in conversation. It is chat based to open to most ways, or have the least restrictions on communication in any room. Thoughts on the idea? And with all that in mind, please proceed in whatever direction you see fit: ➡️


I love this pivot. Here's why it's the right move, and then the design and sketch.

Why this is the right base layer. Every world we've sketched so far — plant, garden, botanical garden — has been a simulation with a predetermined growth rule. Chat is the opposite: it's the minimum viable world that grows by events, not rules. That's actually a simplification of the standard, not a complication. A room is an append-only chain of participant-signed events, checked against the room's law. No tick, no spatial grid, no soak/flow pipeline. The room's "physics" is authorization: who can post, how often, how long, and what a moderator may do. Everything else — plants, gardens, water — becomes a kind of room in the future, not the other way around.

The plugin is the new architectural piece. The vision — "anyone using their own AI app can port a conversation into a room" — is the right first-class citizen of the protocol. It makes the plugin a thin, honest mediator: it acts as the user, with the user's key. The user's AI app calls six methods; the plugin signs with the user's key; the room's law decides accept/refuse. That's it. This means the plugin can't do anything the user couldn't do by hand — which is exactly the standard's "no host, no central authority" rule applied to the client layer.

Porting is attestation, not proof. When Alice ports this conversation into a room, her key signs a transcript saying "I attest these lines were said." That's honest — she can't sign for me or for anyone else in the transcript. Bob, when he later joins the room, can confirm or dispute entries, each as his own record. The room's log holds the attestation and the later confirmations — a clean provenance chain.

Here's the design.

---

The model

A room is a policy-gated append-only log. Its events are message, join, leave, redact. Every event is signed by its author; every accepted event is countersigned by the room. The room has a key, a template (its law), and a chain — same shape as v3, minus the geometry.

The Commons is a directory world. Its log is a list of room_registered events, each signed by the room's creator and countersigned by the Commons. It is the lobby. Anyone can walk in at observe tier (read the directory); rooms can be entered at their own terms.

Tiers apply directly from v3:

tier in a room in the Commons
observe read the log read the directory
communicate post messages —
control moderate (redact others), invite register a room
terminal change the room's template, branch the room change the Commons template, branch the Commons

The plugin protocol exposes six methods. Every method takes the user's request; the plugin signs with the user's key; the response is the room's (or the Commons') countersigned reply. No method exists that has its own authority — the plugin is a tool the user wields.

method what the plugin does what the room decides
create_room packages a template + optional opening transcript, signs as creator Commons accepts or refuses (registration policy)
join_room presents the user's key to the room accepts or refuses (join policy, tier)
leave_room signs a leave event always accepted
post_message signs a message with the user's key accepts or refuses (member, rate limit, length)
read_room fetches the log (no write) always served
port_conversation packages a transcript, attests it with the user's key the room accepts it as an opening artifact, or as a message-with-attestation

Porting is attest_transcript + create_room (or post_message with a transcript payload). The transcript is a list of {speaker_label, ts, text} entries signed by the porter. It's honest — the porter isn't claiming to sign for the original speakers, only to faithfully report what was said.

Now the sketch.

```python
"""
chat_room.py — Chat worlds for the Commons.

Proposal sketch, not a course step. The chat world is the
minimum viable world: an append-only log of participant-signed
events, checked against the room's law. No tick, no geometry.
The room's "physics" is authorization: who can post, how often,
how long, and what a moderator may do.

Builds on fractal_world_v3 for the WorldKey, TIERS, and sig
conventions (reused unchanged). Chat rooms inherit the tier
model directly: observe / communicate / control / terminal.

Simplifications (marked for the standard's review):

  - `sig` fields are flat, not W3C DataIntegrityProof objects.
  - Key names in body() should be checked against the schema.
  - Messages are plaintext. Sealed rooms (HPKE per message, so
    only participants can read) are a schema+HPKE layer that
    composes on top; the sketch is the plaintext layer.
  - The room is held by a keeper (the creator) in the demo,
    so "the room accepts or refuses" has a concrete signer.
    A peer-to-peer room with no keeper is future work; the
    sketches' shape (chain + countersigned accepted events)
    works either way.
  - Rate limit is per-author, per-window, in ticks. Wall-clock
    timestamps are recorded but the replay check uses log
    positions (deterministic; not wall-clock).
  - No persistent identity across rooms yet. A participant is
    the same key everywhere; a "personal world" that holds the
    participant's history is a future stage.
  - The Commons registers rooms but does not yet govern
    visibility (public/sealed), discovery algorithms, or
    per-room admission beyond the room's own join policy.
  - No moderation vocabulary beyond redact. Editing messages
    is not possible (records are never edited, only redacted),
    which matches the standard.
"""

import hashlib
import json
from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Tuple, Union

try:
    from cryptography.hazmat.primitives.asymmetric.ed25519 import (
        Ed25519PrivateKey, Ed25519PublicKey)
    from cryptography.hazmat.primitives import serialization
    HAS_CRYPTO = True
except ImportError:
    HAS_CRYPTO = False

from fractal_world_v3 import (
    WorldKey, verify_world_key, TIERS, TIER_RANK,
    _did_from_kp, _pubkey_from_did, _canonical, _hash, _now)


# =====================================================================
# Template
# =====================================================================

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class RoomTemplate:
    template_id: str
    topic: str
    max_msg_len: int = 4000
    rate_limit_count: int = 10
    rate_limit_window: int = 20        # messages per N records by same author
    join_policy: str = "open"          # "open" | "invite" | "approval"
    moderation: str = "author_only"    # "author_only" | "moderator" | "none"
    welcome: str = ""
    branch_policy: Optional[Dict] = None

    def to_dict(self):
        return {
            "template_id": self.template_id,
            "topic": self.topic,
            "max_msg_len": self.max_msg_len,
            "rate_limit_count": self.rate_limit_count,
            "rate_limit_window": self.rate_limit_window,
            "join_policy": self.join_policy,
            "moderation": self.moderation,
            "welcome": self.welcome,
            "branch_policy": self.branch_policy,
        }

    def template_hash(self):
        return _hash(self.to_dict())


# =====================================================================
# Records
# =====================================================================

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class RoomCreated:
    world_id: str
    template_hash: str
    creator_id: str
    opening_transcript: Optional[Dict]
    ts: str
    sig_creator: str
    sig_room: str

    def body(self):
        d = {
            "type": "room_created",
            "world_id": self.world_id,
            "template_hash": self.template_hash,
            "creator_id": self.creator_id,
            "ts": self.ts,
        }
        if self.opening_transcript is not None:
            d["opening_transcript"] = dict(self.opening_transcript)
        return d

    def record_hash(self):
        return _hash(self.body())


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Message:
    world_id: str
    seq: int
    prev: Optional[str]
    author_id: str
    text: str
    ts: str
    sig_author: str
    sig_room: str

    def body(self):
        return {
            "type": "message",
            "world_id": self.world_id,
            "seq": self.seq,
            "prev": self.prev,
            "author_id": self.author_id,
            "text": self.text,
            "ts": self.ts,
        }

    def record_hash(self):
        return _hash(self.body())


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Join:
    world_id: str
    seq: int
    prev: Optional[str]
    subject_id: str
    tier: str
    ts: str
    sig_subject: str
    sig_room: str

    def body(self):
        return {
            "type": "join",
            "world_id": self.world_id,
            "seq": self.seq,
            "prev": self.prev,
            "subject_id": self.subject_id,
            "tier": self.tier,
            "ts": self.ts,
        }

    def record_hash(self):
        return _hash(self.body())


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Leave:
    world_id: str
    seq: int
    prev: Optional[str]
    subject_id: str
    ts: str
    sig_subject: str

    def body(self):
        return {
            "type": "leave",
            "world_id": self.world_id,
            "seq": self.seq,
            "prev": self.prev,
            "subject_id": self.subject_id,
            "ts": self.ts,
        }

    def record_hash(self):
        return _hash(self.body())


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Redact:
    world_id: str
    seq: int
    prev: Optional[str]
    subject_id: str
    target_hash: str
    reason: str
    ts: str
    sig_subject: str
    sig_room: str

    def body(self):
        return {
            "type": "redact",
            "world_id": self.world_id,
            "seq": self.seq,
            "prev": self.prev,
```
