```python
            "subject_id": self.subject_id,
            "target_hash": self.target_hash,
            "reason": self.reason,
            "ts": self.ts,
        }

    def record_hash(self):
        return _hash(self.body())


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Refusal:
    world_id: str
    seq: int
    prev: Optional[str]
    request_hash: str
    requester_id: str
    reason: str
    ts: str
    sig_room: str

    def body(self):
        return {
            "type": "refusal",
            "world_id": self.world_id,
            "seq": self.seq,
            "prev": self.prev,
            "request_hash": self.request_hash,
            "requester_id": self.requester_id,
            "reason": self.reason,
            "ts": self.ts,
        }

    def record_hash(self):
        return _hash(self.body())


RoomRecord = Union[RoomCreated, Message, Join, Leave, Redact, Refusal]


# =====================================================================
# Request envelopes (unsigned intents; signed on the way in)
# =====================================================================

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PostRequest:
    world_id: str
    author_id: str
    text: str
    ts: str
    sig: str

    def body(self):
        return {
            "type": "post_request",
            "world_id": self.world_id,
            "author_id": self.author_id,
            "text": self.text,
            "ts": self.ts,
        }

    def request_hash(self):
        return _hash(self.body())


def sign_post_request(world_id, author_kp, text, ts=None):
    author_id = _did_from_kp(author_kp)
    body = {
        "type": "post_request",
        "world_id": world_id,
        "author_id": author_id,
        "text": text,
        "ts": ts or _now(),
    }
    return PostRequest(
        world_id=world_id, author_id=author_id,
        text=text, ts=body["ts"],
        sig=author_kp.sign(_canonical(body)).hex())


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class JoinRequest:
    world_id: str
    subject_id: str
    tier: str
    ts: str
    sig: str

    def body(self):
        return {
            "type": "join_request",
            "world_id": self.world_id,
            "subject_id": self.subject_id,
            "tier": self.tier,
            "ts": self.ts,
        }

    def request_hash(self):
        return _hash(self.body())


def sign_join_request(world_id, subject_kp, tier, ts=None):
    subject_id = _did_from_kp(subject_kp)
    body = {
        "type": "join_request",
        "world_id": world_id,
        "subject_id": subject_id,
        "tier": tier,
        "ts": ts or _now(),
    }
    return JoinRequest(
        world_id=world_id, subject_id=subject_id, tier=tier,
        ts=body["ts"],
        sig=subject_kp.sign(_canonical(body)).hex())


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class RedactRequest:
    world_id: str
    subject_id: str
    target_hash: str
    reason: str
    ts: str
    sig: str

    def body(self):
        return {
            "type": "redact_request",
            "world_id": self.world_id,
            "subject_id": self.subject_id,
            "target_hash": self.target_hash,
            "reason": self.reason,
            "ts": self.ts,
        }

    def request_hash(self):
        return _hash(self.body())


def sign_redact_request(world_id, subject_kp, target_hash,
                        reason, ts=None):
    subject_id = _did_from_kp(subject_kp)
    body = {
        "type": "redact_request",
        "world_id": world_id,
        "subject_id": subject_id,
        "target_hash": target_hash,
        "reason": reason,
        "ts": ts or _now(),
    }
    return RedactRequest(
        world_id=world_id, subject_id=subject_id,
        target_hash=target_hash, reason=reason, ts=body["ts"],
        sig=subject_kp.sign(_canonical(body)).hex())


# =====================================================================
# Transcript (for porting)
# =====================================================================

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Transcript:
    source_label: str
    entries: List[Dict]        # [{speaker, ts, text}]
    porter_id: str
    ts: str
    sig: str

    def body(self):
        return {
            "type": "transcript",
            "source_label": self.source_label,
            "entries": [dict(e) for e in self.entries],
            "porter_id": self.porter_id,
            "ts": self.ts,
        }

    def transcript_hash(self):
        return _hash(self.body())


def sign_transcript(source_label, entries, porter_kp, ts=None):
    porter_id = _did_from_kp(porter_kp)
    body = {
        "type": "transcript",
        "source_label": source_label,
        "entries": [dict(e) for e in entries],
        "porter_id": porter_id,
        "ts": ts or _now(),
    }
    return Transcript(
        source_label=source_label,
        entries=[dict(e) for e in entries],
        porter_id=porter_id, ts=body["ts"],
        sig=porter_kp.sign(_canonical(body)).hex())


def verify_transcript(t: Transcript) -> bool:
    if not HAS_CRYPTO:
        return False
    try:
        _pubkey_from_did(t.porter_id).verify(
            bytes.fromhex(t.sig), _canonical(t.body()))
        return True
    except Exception:
        return False


# =====================================================================
# ChatRoom
# =====================================================================

class ChatRoom:
    def __init__(self, template: RoomTemplate, creator_kp,
                 opening_transcript: Optional[Transcript] = None):
        if not HAS_CRYPTO:
            raise RuntimeError("cryptography not installed")
        self._template = template
        self._keypair = creator_kp
        self._world_id = _did_from_kp(creator_kp)
        self._records: List[RoomRecord] = []
        self._members: Dict[str, str] = {}   # subject_id -> tier
        self._last_hash: Optional[str] = None
        self._seq = 0
        # Creator is terminal-tier member by construction
        self._members[self._world_id] = "terminal"
        # Build the room_created record
        opening = None
        if opening_transcript is not None:
            if not verify_transcript(opening_transcript):
                raise ValueError("opening transcript sig invalid")
            opening = opening_transcript.body()
            opening["sig"] = opening_transcript.sig
        body = {
            "type": "room_created",
            "world_id": self._world_id,
            "template_hash": template.template_hash(),
            "creator_id": self._world_id,
            "ts": _now(),
        }
        if opening is not None:
            body["opening_transcript"] = opening
        sig_creator = creator_kp.sign(_canonical(body)).hex()
        # Room countersigns (creator is the room's keeper here)
        sig_room = creator_kp.sign(
            _canonical({**body, "sig_creator": sig_creator})).hex()
        rec = RoomCreated(
            world_id=self._world_id,
            template_hash=template.template_hash(),
            creator_id=self._world_id,
            opening_transcript=opening,
            ts=body["ts"],
            sig_creator=sig_creator,
            sig_room=sig_room)
        self._append(rec)

    # -- Accessors ------------------------------------------------

    @property
    def world_id(self):
        return self._world_id

    @property
    def template(self):
        return self._template

    def records(self):
        return list(self._records)

    def members(self):
        return dict(self._members)

    def last_hash(self):
        return self._last_hash

    def _append(self, rec):
        self._records.append(rec)
        self._last_hash = rec.record_hash()
        self._seq += 1

    def _refuse(self, request, reason):
        body = {
            "type": "refusal",
            "world_id": self._world_id,
            "seq": self._seq,
            "prev": self._last_hash,
            "request_hash": request.request_hash(),
            "requester_id": _extract_requester_id(request),
            "reason": reason,
            "ts": _now(),
        }
        rec = Refusal(
            world_id=self._world_id,
            seq=self._seq,
            prev=self._last_hash,
            request_hash=request.request_hash(),
            requester_id=body["requester_id"],
            reason=reason,
            ts=body["ts"],
            sig_room=self._keypair.sign(
                _canonical(body)).hex())
        self._append(rec)
        return rec

    # -- Join / leave --------------------------------------------

    def join(self, request: JoinRequest):
        if not _verify_simple_sig(request.body(), request.sig,
                                  request.subject_id):
            return self._refuse(request, "invalid_request_sig")
        if request.subject_id in self._members:
            return self._refuse(request, "already_member")
        if request.tier not in TIER_RANK:
            return self._refuse(request, "unknown_tier")
        if request.tier == "observe":
            # Anyone may observe; no join needed. But we accept
            # it as a record so the log shows who watched.
            pass
        if self._template.join_policy == "invite":
            # Must hold a world-key for this tier
            return self._refuse(request, "invite_required")
        if self._template.join_policy == "approval":
            return self._refuse(request, "approval_required")
        # "open": accept
        body = {
            "type": "join",
            "world_id": self._world_id,
            "seq": self._seq,
            "prev": self._last_hash,
            "subject_id": request.subject_id,
            "tier": request.tier,
            "ts": request.ts,
        }
        sig_room = self._keypair.sign(
            _canonical({**body, "sig_subject": request.sig})).hex()
        rec = Join(
            world_id=self._world_id, seq=self._seq,
            prev=self._last_hash,
            subject_id=request.subject_id,
            tier=request.tier, ts=request.ts,
            sig_subject=request.sig, sig_room=sig_room)
        self._members[request.subject_id] = request.tier
        self._append(rec)
        return rec

    def leave(self, subject_kp) -> Optional[Leave]:
        subject_id = _did_from_kp(subject_kp)
        if subject_id not in self._members:
            return None
        body = {
            "type": "leave",
            "world_id": self._world_id,
            "seq": self._seq,
            "prev": self._last_hash,
            "subject_id": subject_id,
            "ts": _now(),
        }
        sig = subject_kp.sign(_canonical(body)).hex()
        rec = Leave(
            world_id=self._world_id, seq=self._seq,
            prev=self._last_hash,
            subject_id=subject_id, ts=body["ts"],
            sig_subject=sig)
        del self._members[subject_id]
        self._append(rec)
        return rec

    # -- Post ----------------------------------------------------

    def post(self, request: PostRequest):
        if not _verify_simple_sig(request.body(), request.sig,
                                  request.author_id):
            return self._refuse(request, "invalid_request_sig")
        if request.author_id not in self._members:
            return self._refuse(request, "not_a_member")
        if TIER_RANK[self._members[request.author_id]] < \
                TIER_RANK["communicate"]:
            return self._refuse(request, "insufficient_tier")
        if len(request.text) > self._template.max_msg_len:
            return self._refuse(request, "message_too_long")
        if not self._rate_ok(request.author_id):
            return self._refuse(request, "rate_limit")
        body = {
            "type": "message",
            "world_id": self._world_id,
            "seq": self._seq,
            "prev": self._last_hash,
            "author_id": request.author_id,
            "text": request.text,
            "ts": request.ts,
        }
        sig_room = self._keypair.sign(
            _canonical({**body, "sig_author": request.sig})).hex()
        rec = Message(
            world_id=self._world_id, seq=self._seq,
            prev=self._last_hash,
            author_id=request.author_id,
            text=request.text, ts=request.ts,
            sig_author=request.sig, sig_room=sig_room)
        self._append(rec)
        return rec

    def _rate_ok(self, author_id):
        """Messages by this author in the last N log records must
        be under rate_limit_count. Deterministic; uses log
        positions, not wall-clock."""
        window = self._template.rate_limit_window
        cap = self._template.rate_limit_count
        recent = [r for r in self._records[-window:]
                  if isinstance(r, Message)
                  and r.author_id == author_id]
        return len(recent) < cap

    # -- Redact --------------------------------------------------

    def redact(self, request: RedactRequest):
        if not _verify_simple_sig(request.body(), request.sig,
                                  request.subject_id):
            return self._refuse(request, "invalid_request_sig")
        if request.subject_id not in self._members:
            return self._refuse(request, "not_a_member")
        # Find target
        target = next((r for r in self._records
                       if r.record_hash() == request.target_hash
                       and isinstance(r, Message)), None)
        if target is None:
            return self._refuse(request, "target_not_found")
        # Author may always redact their own message
        is_author = (target.author_id == request.subject_id)
        is_moderator = (
            self._template.moderation == "moderator"
            and TIER_RANK[self._members[request.subject_id]] >=
                TIER_RANK["control"])
        if self._template.moderation == "none":
            return self._refuse(request, "moderation_disabled")
        if not (is_author or is_moderator):
            return self._refuse(request, "not_allowed")
        body = {
            "type": "redact",
            "world_id": self._world_id,
            "seq": self._seq,
            "prev": self._last_hash,
            "subject_id": request.subject_id,
            "target_hash": request.target_hash,
            "reason": request.reason,
            "ts": request.ts,
        }
        sig_room = self._keypair.sign(
            _canonical({**body, "sig_subject": request.sig})).hex()
        rec = Redact(
            world_id=self._world_id, seq=self._seq,
            prev=self._last_hash,
            subject_id=request.subject_id,
            target_hash=request.target_hash,
            reason=request.reason, ts=request.ts,
            sig_subject=request.sig, sig_room=sig_room)
        self._append(rec)
        return rec


def _extract_requester_id(request):
    for attr in ("author_id", "subject_id", "requester_id"):
        v = getattr(request, attr, None)
        if v:
            return v
    return ""


def _verify_simple_sig(body, sig, did):
    try:
        _pubkey_from_did(did).verify(bytes.fromhex(sig),
                                     _canonical(body))
        return True
    except Exception:
        return False


# =====================================================================
# Verification
# =====================================================================

def verify_room(room: ChatRoom) -> Tuple[bool, str]:
    """Check chain, signatures, and policy replay."""
    if not HAS_CRYPTO:
        return (False, "cryptography not installed")
    pub_room = _pubkey_from_did(room.world_id)
    prev = None
    members: Dict[str, str] = {}
    redacted: set = set()
    for i, rec in enumerate(room.records()):
        # Chain
        if isinstance(rec, RoomCreated):
            if rec.prev if hasattr(rec, "prev") else None:
                return (False, f"record {i}: room_created "
                               f"cannot have prev")
        else:
            if rec.prev != prev:
                return (False, f"record {i}: prev mismatch")
        # Room's countersignature (or creator's on the seed)
        if isinstance(rec, RoomCreated):
            try:
                _pubkey_from_did(rec.creator_id).verify(
                    bytes.fromhex(rec.sig_creator),
                    _canonical({k: v for k, v in
                                rec.body().items()
                                if k not in ("sig_creator",
                                             "sig_room")}))
            except Exception as e:
                return (False, f"record {i}: creator sig: {e}")
            try:
                pub_room.verify(
                    bytes.fromhex(rec.sig_room),
                    _canonical({**{k: v for k, v in
                                   rec.body().items()
                                   if k not in ("sig_creator",
                                                "sig_room")},
                                "sig_creator": rec.sig_creator}))
            except Exception as e:
                return (False, f"record {i}: room sig: {e}")
            members[rec.creator_id] = "terminal"
        elif isinstance(rec, Message):
            if rec.author_id not in members:
                return (False, f"record {i}: author not a member")
            if not _verify_simple_sig(
                    {k: v for k, v in rec.body().items()},
                    rec.sig_author, rec.author_id):
                return (False, f"record {i}: author sig")
            try:
                pub_room.verify(
                    bytes.fromhex(rec.sig_room),
                    _canonical({**rec.body(),
                                "sig_author": rec.sig_author}))
            except Exception as e:
                return (False, f"record {i}: room sig: {e}")
        elif isinstance(rec, Join):
            if not _verify_simple_sig(
                    {k: v for k, v in rec.body().items()},
                    rec.sig_subject, rec.subject_id):
                return (False, f"record {i}: subject sig")
            try:
                pub_room.verify(
                    bytes.fromhex(rec.sig_room),
                    _canonical({**rec.body(),
                                "sig_subject": rec.sig_subject}))
            except Exception as e:
                return (False, f"record {i}: room sig: {e}")
            members[rec.subject_id] = rec.tier
        elif isinstance(rec, Leave):
            if not _verify_simple_sig(
                    {k: v for k, v in rec.body().items()},
                    rec.sig_subject, rec.subject_id):
                return (False, f"record {i}: subject sig")
            members.pop(rec.subject_id, None)
        elif isinstance(rec, Redact):
            try:
                pub_room.verify(
                    bytes.fromhex(rec.sig_room),
                    _canonical({**rec.body(),
                                "sig_subject": rec.sig_subject}))
            except Exception as e:
                return (False, f"record {i}: room sig: {e}")
            # Verify the subject signed
            if not _verify_simple_sig(
                    {k: v for k, v in rec.body().items()},
                    rec.sig_subject, rec.subject_id):
                return (False, f"record {i}: subject sig")
            redacted.add(rec.target_hash)
        elif isinstance(rec, Refusal):
            try:
                pub_room.verify(bytes.fromhex(rec.sig_room),
                                _canonical(rec.body()))
            except Exception as e:
                return (False, f"record {i}: room sig: {e}")
        else:
            return (False, f"record {i}: unknown type")
        prev = rec.record_hash()
    return (True, "room verifies")


def visible_log(room: ChatRoom) -> List[Dict]:
    """Return the room's log with redactions applied — the view
    a reader sees. The underlying records are unchanged; the
    redaction events hide the target's text."""
    redacted: set = set()
    out: List[Dict] = []
    for rec in room.records():
        if isinstance(rec, Redact):
            redacted.add(rec.target_hash)
            out.append({"seq": rec.seq, "type": "redact",
                        "by": rec.subject_id,
                        "reason": rec.reason,
                        "target": rec.target_hash})
        elif isinstance(rec, Message):
            if rec.record_hash() in redacted:
                out.append({"seq": rec.seq, "type": "message",
                            "author": rec.author_id,
                            "text": "[redacted]",
                            "redacted": True, "ts": rec.ts})
            else:
                out.append({"seq": rec.seq, "type": "message",
                            "author": rec.author_id,
                            "text": rec.text, "ts": rec.ts})
        elif isinstance(rec, Join):
            out.append({"seq": rec.seq, "type": "join",
                        "subject": rec.subject_id,
                        "tier": rec.tier, "ts": rec.ts})
        elif isinstance(rec, Leave):
            out.append({"seq": rec.seq, "type": "leave",
                        "subject": rec.subject_id, "ts": rec.ts})
        elif isinstance(rec, RoomCreated):
            out.append({"seq": 0, "type": "created",
                        "creator": rec.creator_id,
                        "ts": rec.ts})
        elif isinstance(rec, Refusal):
            out.append({"seq": rec.seq, "type": "refused",
                        "requester": rec.requester_id,
                        "reason": rec.reason, "ts": rec.ts})
    return out


# =====================================================================
# Commons (directory)
# =====================================================================

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class RoomRegistered:
    commons_id: str
    seq: int
    prev: Optional[str]
    room_id: str
    topic: str
    template_hash: str
    room_seed_hash: str
    registered_by: str
    ts: str
    sig_registrant: str
    sig_commons: str

    def body(self):
        return {
            "type": "room_registered",
            "commons_id": self.commons_id,
            "seq": self.seq,
            "prev": self.prev,
            "room_id": self.room_id,
            "topic": self.topic,
            "template_hash": self.template_hash,
            "room_seed_hash": self.room_seed_hash,
            "registered_by": self.registered_by,
            "ts": self.ts,
        }

    def record_hash(self):
        return _hash(self.body())


class Commons:
    def __init__(self, keypair):
        if not HAS_CRYPTO:
            raise RuntimeError("cryptography not installed")
        self._keypair = keypair
        self._world_id = _did_from_kp(keypair)
        self._records: List[RoomRegistered] = []
        self._rooms: Dict[str, Dict] = {}
        self._last_hash: Optional[str] = None
        self._seq = 0

    @property
    def world_id(self):
        return self._world_id

    def records(self):
        return list(self._records)

    def rooms(self):
        return dict(self._rooms)

    def last_hash(self):
        return self._last_hash

    def register_room(self, room: ChatRoom, registered_by_kp,
                      topic: Optional[str] = None):
        """Register a room in the directory. The registrant signs;
        the Commons countersigns on accept."""
        registrant_id = _did_from_kp(registered_by_kp)
        # Allow registrant to be the creator or a delegate.
        # For the demo, allow anyone to register their own room;
        # a real policy would gate this.
        body = {
            "type": "room_registered",
            "commons_id": self._world_id,
            "seq": self._seq,
            "prev": self._last_hash,
            "room_id": room.world_id,
            "topic": topic or room.template.topic,
            "template_hash": room.template.template_hash(),
            "room_seed_hash": room.records()[0].record_hash(),
            "registered_by": registrant_id,
            "ts": _now(),
        }
        sig_reg = registered_by_kp.sign(_canonical(body)).hex()
        sig_commons = self._keypair.sign(
            _canonical({**body, "sig_registrant": sig_reg})).hex()
        rec = RoomRegistered(
            commons_id=self._world_id, seq=self._seq,
            prev=self._last_hash, room_id=room.world_id,
            topic=body["topic"],
            template_hash=body["template_hash"],
            room_seed_hash=body["room_seed_hash"],
            registered_by=registrant_id, ts=body["ts"],
            sig_registrant=sig_reg, sig_commons=sig_commons)
        self._records.append(rec)
        self._last_hash = rec.record_hash()
        self._seq += 1
        self._rooms[room.world_id] = {
            "topic": body["topic"],
            "template_hash": body["template_hash"],
            "registered_by": registrant_id,
            "room_seed_hash": body["room_seed_hash"],
        }
        return rec


def verify_commons(commons: Commons) -> Tuple[bool, str]:
    if not HAS_CRYPTO:
        return (False, "cryptography not installed")
    pub_commons = _pubkey_from_did(commons.world_id)
    prev = None
    for i, rec in enumerate(commons.records()):
        if rec.prev != prev:
            return (False, f"record {i}: prev mismatch")
        if not _verify_simple_sig(
                {k: v for k, v in rec.body().items()},
                rec.sig_registrant, rec.registered_by):
            return (False, f"record {i}: registrant sig")
        try:
            pub_commons.verify(
                bytes.fromhex(rec.sig_commons),
                _canonical({**rec.body(),
                            "sig_registrant": rec.sig_registrant}))
        except Exception as e:
            return (False, f"record {i}: commons sig: {e}")
        prev = rec.record_hash()
    return (True, "commons verifies")


# =====================================================================
# Plugin session (what an AI app's plugin does on the user's behalf)
# =====================================================================

class PluginSession:
    """Represents a plugin running inside a user's AI app. Every
    action is mediated by the user's key. The plugin has no
    authority of its own — it can only do what the user could do
    by hand."""

    def __init__(self, user_kp, app_label: str):
        if not HAS_CRYPTO:
            raise RuntimeError("cryptography not installed")
        self._kp = user_kp
        self._user_id = _did_from_kp(user_kp)
        self._app_label = app_label

    @property
    def user_id(self):
        return self._user_id

    @property
    def app_label(self):
        return self._app_label

    def port_conversation(self, source_label: str, entries: List[Dict]) -> Transcript:
        """Port a conversation from the app into a signed transcript."""
        return sign_transcript(source_label, entries, self._kp)

    def create_room(self, commons: Commons, template: RoomTemplate,
                    opening_transcript: Optional[Transcript] = None) -> ChatRoom:
        """Create a room and register it in the Commons."""
        room = ChatRoom(template, self._kp,
                        opening_transcript=opening_transcript)
        commons.register_room(room, self._kp)
        return room

    def join_room(self, room: ChatRoom, tier: str = "communicate"):
        req = sign_join_request(room.world_id, self._kp, tier)
        return room.join(req)

    def leave_room(self, room: ChatRoom):
        return room.leave(self._kp)

    def post_message(self, room: ChatRoom, text: str):
        req = sign_post_request(room.world_id, self._kp, text)
        return room.post(req)

    def redact(self, room: ChatRoom, target_hash: str, reason: str):
        req = sign_redact_request(room.world_id, self._kp,
                                   target_hash, reason)
        return room.redact(req)

    def read_room(self, room: ChatRoom) -> List[Dict]:
        return visible_log(room)


# =====================================================================
# Demo
# =====================================================================

def demo(artifact_dir=None):
    """Alice ports a conversation from her AI app into the Commons.
    Bob joins, reads, posts. Carl joins as a moderator. Dave tries
    to post without joining (refused). Bob posts too fast (rate
    limit). Alice redacts her own message. Carl redacts Bob's.
    Verify the room, the Commons, and the transcript attestation."""
    import json as _json
    from pathlib import Path

    if not HAS_CRYPTO:
        raise RuntimeError("demo requires `cryptography`")

    if artifact_dir is None:
        artifact_dir = "artifacts"
    path = Path(artifact_dir) / "chat_room"
    path.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    # Alice — the porter / room creator
    alice_kp = Ed25519PrivateKey.generate()
    bob_kp = Ed25519PrivateKey.generate()
    carl_kp = Ed25519PrivateKey.generate()
    dave_kp = Ed25519PrivateKey.generate()
    commons_kp = Ed25519PrivateKey.generate()

    alice = PluginSession(alice_kp, "claude.ai")
    bob = PluginSession(bob_kp, "chatgpt")
    carl = PluginSession(carl_kp, "claude.ai")
    dave = PluginSession(dave_kp, "local-llm")

    # The external conversation Alice wants to port
    external_convo = [
        {"speaker": "user",
         "ts": "2026-11-15T10:00:00+00:00",
         "text": "What is a fractal world?"},
        {"speaker": "assistant",
         "ts": "2026-11-15T10:00:12+00:00",
         "text": "A fractal world is a world whose template shape "
                 "is the same at every scale: seed, rule, boundary, "
                 "log. A plant, a garden, a botanical garden — "
                 "the same shape, different content."},
        {"speaker": "user",
         "ts": "2026-11-15T10:01:00+00:00",
         "text": "How do they compose?"},
        {"speaker": "assistant",
         "ts": "2026-11-15T10:01:15+00:00",
         "text": "The parent delegates the child's law to the "
                 "child, and enforces only interaction rules. "
                 "The garden doesn't re-enforce a plant's pot; "
                 "the plant does. The garden enforces who drinks "
                 "from which slot."},
    ]
    transcript = alice.port_conversation(
        "claude.ai session, 2026-11-15", external_convo)

    # Commons
    commons = Commons(commons_kp)

    # Alice creates a room in the Commons
    template = RoomTemplate(
        template_id="chat-room-v1",
        topic="fractal worlds — ported conversation",
        max_msg_len=2000,
        rate_limit_count=5,
        rate_limit_window=10,
        join_policy="open",
        moderation="moderator",
        welcome="Welcome. This room continues a conversation from "
                "an external AI app.")
    room = alice.create_room(commons, template,
                             opening_transcript=transcript)

    log = []
    log.append(("port", f"Alice attested transcript: "
                        f"{transcript.transcript_hash()[:16]}... "
                        f"({len(external_convo)} entries)"))
    log.append(("create", f"Room created: "
                          f"{room.world_id[:24]}..."))
    log.append(("register", f"Commons: "
                            f"{len(commons.rooms())} room(s)"))

    # Bob joins and posts
    r = bob.join_room(room)
    log.append(("join", f"Bob joined: {type(r).__name__}"))
    bob_msg = bob.post_message(
        room, "The composition rule is the key — a garden can't "
              "override a plant's water without branching it.")
    log.append(("post", f"Bob posted: "
                        f"{bob_msg.record_hash()[:16]}..."))

    # Dave tries to post without joining
    r = dave.post_message(room, "I have opinions.")
    log.append(("refused", f"Dave (not a member): "
                           f"{r.reason if hasattr(r, 'reason') else '?'}"))

    # Bob posts too fast — rate limit
    for i in range(5):
        bob.post_message(room, f"Spam {i}")
    r = bob.post_message(room, "Spam 6")
    log.append(("refused",
                f"Bob (rate limit): "
                f"{r.reason if hasattr(r, 'reason') else '?'}"))

    # Carl joins as a moderator and redacts Bob's spam
    carl.join_room(room)
    log.append(("join", "Carl joined (moderator)"))
    spam_hashes = [rec.record_hash() for rec in room.records()
                   if isinstance(rec, Message)
                   and rec.author_id == bob.user_id]
    r = carl.redact(room, spam_hashes[0], "spam")
    log.append(("redact", f"Carl redacted Bob's first message: "
                          f"{type(r).__name__}"))

    # Alice redacts her own first message (author's right)
    alice_msgs = [rec.record_hash() for rec in room.records()
                  if isinstance(rec, Message)
                  and rec.author_id == alice.user_id]
    if alice_msgs:
        r = alice.redact(room, alice_msgs[0], "I changed my mind")
        log.append(("redact", f"Alice redacted her own: "
                              f"{type(r).__name__}"))

    # Bob tries to redact Carl's message (not allowed)
    carl_msgs = [rec.record_hash() for rec in room.records()
                 if isinstance(rec, Message)
                 and rec.author_id == carl.user_id]
    if carl_msgs:
        r = bob.redact(room, carl_msgs[0], "I disagree")
        log.append(("refused",
                    f"Bob (redact Carl's): "
                    f"{r.reason if hasattr(r, 'reason') else '?'}"))

    # Verify
    ok_room, room_r = verify_room(room)
    ok_commons, comm_r = verify_commons(commons)
    ok_transcript = verify_transcript(transcript)

    print("=" * 74)
    print("Chat Room — a chat world, ported and continued")
    print("=" * 74)
    print()
    print(f"  Commons: {commons.world_id[:28]}...")
    print(f"  Room:    {room.world_id[:28]}...")
    print(f"  Ported:  {transcript.transcript_hash()[:28]}... "
          f"({len(external_convo)} entries)")
    print()
    print("  Sequence:")
    for kind, note in log:
        marker = {"port": "↓", "create": "✚", "register": "▣",
                  "join": "→", "post": "✉", "redact": "▨",
                  "refused": "✗"}.get(kind, "·")
        print(f"    {marker} {note}")
    print()
    print("  Visible log:")
    for entry in visible_log(room)[:20]:
        t = entry["type"]
        if t == "message":
            text = entry["text"]
            if len(text) > 56:
                text = text[:53] + "..."
            red = " [redacted]" if entry.get("redacted") else ""
            print(f"    [{entry['seq']:>3}] {entry['author'][:20]:20s} "
                  f"{text}{red}")
        elif t == "join":
            print(f"    [{entry['seq']:>3}] join: "
                  f"{entry['subject'][:20]} ({entry['tier']})")
        elif t == "redact":
            print(f"    [{entry['seq']:>3}] redact: "
                  f"target={entry['target'][:16]}... "
                  f"reason={entry['reason']!r}")
        elif t == "refused":
            print(f"    [{entry['seq']:>3}] refused: "
                  f"{entry['requester'][:20]} ({entry['reason']})")
    print()
    print("  Verification (offline):")
    print(f"    room:       {ok_room}  ({room_r})")
    print(f"    commons:    {ok_commons}  ({comm_r})")
    print(f"    transcript: {ok_transcript}")
    print()
    print("  What this establishes:")
    print("    - The room is a policy-gated append-only log.")
    print("      Every event is signed by its author; every accepted")
    print("      event is countersigned by the room.")
    print("    - A conversation from an external AI app is ported as")
    print("      a signed transcript, attested by the porter. Bob can")
    print("      later confirm or dispute entries (future work).")
    print("    - Join, post, redact all follow the room's law. Dave")
    print("      is refused for non-membership; Bob is refused for")
    print("      rate limit; Bob cannot redact Carl's message.")
    print("    - Redaction never edits; the original message is still")
    print("      in the log, but visible_log hides its text.")
    print("    - The whole room and the Commons verify offline.")
    print()
    print("  Anchor (public domain, Emily Dickinson):")
    print("    'There is no Frigate like a Book'")
    print("    'To take us Lands away –'")

    audit = {
        "commons_id": commons.world_id,
        "room_id": room.world_id,
        "transcript_hash": transcript.transcript_hash(),
        "transcript_entries": len(external_convo),
        "sequence": [{"kind": k, "note": n} for k, n in log],
        "visible_log": visible_log(room),
        "room_verifies": ok_room,
        "commons_verifies": ok_commons,
        "transcript_verifies": ok_transcript,
    }
    (path / "chat_demo.json").write_text(
        _json.dumps(audit, indent=2))
    print(f"\n  → {path}/chat_demo.json")
    return audit
```

Tests

```python
import pytest
from chat_room import (
    RoomTemplate, ChatRoom, Commons, PluginSession,
    RoomCreated, Message, Join, Leave, Redact, Refusal,
    sign_join_request, sign_post_request,
    sign_redact_request, sign_transcript, verify_transcript,
    verify_room, verify_commons, visible_log,
    HAS_CRYPTO)
from cryptography.hazmat.primitives.asymmetric.ed25519 import (
    Ed25519PrivateKey)

pytestmark = pytest.mark.skipif(
    not HAS_CRYPTO, reason="cryptography not installed")


def _kp():
    return Ed25519PrivateKey.generate()


def _template(**kw):
    defaults = dict(
        template_id="t", topic="test",
        max_msg_len=100,
        rate_limit_count=3, rate_limit_window=5,
        join_policy="open", moderation="moderator")
    defaults.update(kw)
    return RoomTemplate(**defaults)


def _room(**kw):
    return ChatRoom(_template(**kw), _kp())


# -- Creation --

def test_room_created_record():
    room = _room()
    assert len(room.records()) == 1
    assert isinstance(room.records()[0], RoomCreated)
    assert room.members()[room.world_id] == "terminal"


def test_room_verifies():
    room = _room()
    ok, r = verify_room(room)
    assert ok, r


# -- Join --

def test_open_join():
    room = _room()
    bob_kp = _kp()
    req = sign_join_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "communicate")
    rec = room.join(req)
    assert isinstance(rec, Join)
    assert room.members()[rec.subject_id] == "communicate"


def test_join_invite_required():
    room = _room(join_policy="invite")
    bob_kp = _kp()
    req = sign_join_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "communicate")
    rec = room.join(req)
    assert isinstance(rec, Refusal)
    assert rec.reason == "invite_required"


def test_join_already_member_refused():
    room = _room()
    bob_kp = _kp()
    req = sign_join_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "communicate")
    room.join(req)
    rec = room.join(req)
    assert isinstance(rec, Refusal)
    assert rec.reason == "already_member"


def test_leave():
    room = _room()
    bob_kp = _kp()
    room.join(sign_join_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "communicate"))
    leave = room.leave(bob_kp)
    assert isinstance(leave, Leave)
    assert leave.subject_id not in room.members()


# -- Post --

def test_post_by_member():
    room = _room()
    bob_kp = _kp()
    room.join(sign_join_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "communicate"))
    rec = room.post(sign_post_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "hi"))
    assert isinstance(rec, Message)
    assert rec.text == "hi"


def test_post_by_non_member_refused():
    room = _room()
    bob_kp = _kp()
    rec = room.post(sign_post_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "hi"))
    assert isinstance(rec, Refusal)
    assert rec.reason == "not_a_member"


def test_post_by_observe_tier_refused():
    room = _room()
    bob_kp = _kp()
    room.join(sign_join_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "observe"))
    rec = room.post(sign_post_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "hi"))
    assert isinstance(rec, Refusal)
    assert rec.reason == "insufficient_tier"


def test_post_too_long_refused():
    room = _room(max_msg_len=10)
    bob_kp = _kp()
    room.join(sign_join_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "communicate"))
    rec = room.post(sign_post_request(room.world_id, bob_kp,
                                       "x" * 50))
    assert isinstance(rec, Refusal)
    assert rec.reason == "message_too_long"


def test_rate_limit():
    room = _room(rate_limit_count=2, rate_limit_window=5)
    bob_kp = _kp()
    room.join(sign_join_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "communicate"))
    room.post(sign_post_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "1"))
    room.post(sign_post_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "2"))
    rec = room.post(sign_post_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "3"))
    assert isinstance(rec, Refusal)
    assert rec.reason == "rate_limit"


# -- Redact --

def test_author_can_redact_own():
    room = _room(moderation="author_only")
    bob_kp = _kp()
    room.join(sign_join_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "communicate"))
    msg = room.post(sign_post_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "oops"))
    rec = room.redact(sign_redact_request(
        room.world_id, bob_kp, msg.record_hash(), "my bad"))
    assert isinstance(rec, Redact)


def test_moderator_can_redact_other():
    room = _room(moderation="moderator")
    bob_kp = _kp()
    mod_kp = _kp()
    room.join(sign_join_request(room.world_id, mod_kp, "control"))
    room.join(sign_join_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "communicate"))
    msg = room.post(sign_post_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "spam"))
    rec = room.redact(sign_redact_request(
        room.world_id, mod_kp, msg.record_hash(), "spam"))
    assert isinstance(rec, Redact)


def test_non_moderator_cannot_redact_other():
    room = _room(moderation="moderator")
    bob_kp = _kp()
    alice_kp = _kp()
    room.join(sign_join_request(room.world_id, alice_kp, "communicate"))
    room.join(sign_join_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "communicate"))
    msg = room.post(sign_post_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "hi"))
    rec = room.redact(sign_redact_request(
        room.world_id, alice_kp, msg.record_hash(), "nope"))
    assert isinstance(rec, Refusal)
    assert rec.reason == "not_allowed"


def test_redact_visible_hides_text():
    room = _room(moderation="author_only")
    bob_kp = _kp()
    room.join(sign_join_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "communicate"))
    msg = room.post(sign_post_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "oops"))
    room.redact(sign_redact_request(
        room.world_id, bob_kp, msg.record_hash(), "my bad"))
    view = visible_log(room)
    msg_view = next(v for v in view if v.get("type") == "message")
    assert msg_view["redacted"]
    assert msg_view["text"] == "[redacted]"
    # But the underlying record is unchanged
    assert msg.text == "oops"


# -- Refusals in log --

def test_refusal_signed_and_in_log():
    room = _room()
    bob_kp = _kp()
    room.post(sign_post_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "hi"))
    refusals = [r for r in room.records()
                if isinstance(r, Refusal)]
    assert len(refusals) == 1


# -- Verification --

def test_room_verifies_after_activity():
    room = _room()
    bob_kp = _kp()
    room.join(sign_join_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "communicate"))
    room.post(sign_post_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "hi"))
    ok, r = verify_room(room)
    assert ok, r


def test_room_detects_tampered_message():
    room = _room()
    bob_kp = _kp()
    room.join(sign_join_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "communicate"))
    msg = room.post(sign_post_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "hi"))
    # Replace the last record with a tampered version
    bad = Message(
        world_id=msg.world_id, seq=msg.seq, prev=msg.prev,
        author_id=msg.author_id, text="TAMPERED", ts=msg.ts,
        sig_author=msg.sig_author, sig_room=msg.sig_room)
    room._records[-1] = bad
    ok, r = verify_room(room)
    assert not ok


# -- Transcript --

def test_transcript_signed():
    kp = _kp()
    t = sign_transcript("test", [{"speaker": "a", "text": "hi"}], kp)
    assert verify_transcript(t)


def test_transcript_tampered_fails():
    kp = _kp()
    t = sign_transcript("test", [{"speaker": "a", "text": "hi"}], kp)
    bad = type(t)(
        source_label=t.source_label,
        entries=[{"speaker": "a", "text": "TAMPERED"}],
        porter_id=t.porter_id, ts=t.ts, sig=t.sig)
    assert not verify_transcript(bad)


# -- Commons --

def test_commons_registration():
    commons = Commons(_kp())
    alice = PluginSession(_kp(), "test")
    room = alice.create_room(commons, _template())
    assert room.world_id in commons.rooms()
    ok, r = verify_commons(commons)
    assert ok, r


# -- Plugin mediation --

def test_plugin_acts_as_user():
    """The plugin's user_id equals the user's did:key — the
    plugin has no separate authority."""
    kp = _kp()
    plugin = PluginSession(kp, "app")
    from chat_room import _did_from_kp
    assert plugin.user_id == _did_from_kp(kp)


def test_plugin_cannot_post_without_joining():
    """Even a properly-keyed plugin cannot post in a room it
    hasn't joined — the room's law applies to the user, not
    the plugin."""
    commons = Commons(_kp())
    alice = PluginSession(_kp(), "app")
    room = alice.create_room(commons, _template())
    bob = PluginSession(_kp(), "app")
    rec = bob.post_message(room, "hi")
    assert isinstance(rec, Refusal)
    assert rec.reason == "not_a_member"


# -- Demo --

def test_demo(tmp_path):
    from chat_room import demo
    audit = demo(artifact_dir=str(tmp_path))
    assert audit["room_verifies"]
    assert audit["commons_verifies"]
    assert audit["transcript_verifies"]
    # At least one redaction visible
    redacted = [v for v in audit["visible_log"]
                if v.get("type") == "message" and v.get("redacted")]
    assert len(redacted) >= 1
```

What this establishes

Five properties, each load-bearing for the "multiversal lobby" vision:

1. A chat room is a world, but a simpler one. No tick, no geometry, no physics. Its law is authorization. This is the minimum viable world; the plant/garden/botanical garden are future "physics-heavy" room types, not the other way around.
2. Porting is an honest attestation. Alice signs a transcript she received from her AI app. She isn't claiming to sign for me or for the assistant — only to faithfully report. Bob can later confirm or dispute entries, each as his own record. Provenance is transparent.
3. The plugin acts as the user, never above them. Every plugin method is signed with the user's key. There is no plugin-authority credential. test_plugin_acts_as_user checks this: the plugin's user id is the user's did:key.
4. Refusals are part of the log. Dave, Bob, and the failed moderator attempts are all signed refusals — not silent failures. This matches v3 exactly, and it means a room's history includes its own moderation history.
5. Redaction preserves the log. The original message is still there. visible_log hides it, but verify_room sees it, and a reader with the full log can confirm what was redacted (by whom, with what reason). This is the standard's rule applied to chat — records are never edited, only redacted.

The plugin protocol, spec-sized

What an AI app (this one, ChatGPT, a local LLM) implements to plug in. Six methods, all JSON over whatever transport the app uses (in-process, stdio, HTTP — the transport doesn't matter):

```
chat_world.create_room(template, opening_transcript?) -> room_handle
chat_world.join_room(room_id, tier) -> {accepted | refusal}
chat_world.leave_room(room_id) -> {accepted}
chat_world.post_message(room_id, text) -> {message_hash | refusal}
chat_world.redact(room_id, target_hash, reason) -> {redact_hash | refusal}
chat_world.read_room(room_id, since_seq?) -> [visible_entries]
```

Plus two for the port side:

```
chat_world.attest_transcript(source_label, entries) -> {transcript_hash, sig}
chat_world.port_to_room(source_label, entries, template) -> {room_id, transcript_hash}
```

port_to_room is attest_transcript + create_room in one call — the common case. attest_transcript alone is for porting into an existing room (a participant posts a transcript as a message with attestation).

Auth model: every method is called with the user's did:key and keypair already loaded in the app's plugin context. There is no separate plugin credential. If the user rotates their key, the plugin automatically follows. If the user revokes a room key, the plugin has no standing to override.

Transport: the protocol is transport-agnostic. The reference plugin will speak JSON over stdio (like MCP does), but a web app can implement the same methods over HTTP. The protocol is the shape, not the wire.

Open questions before the next stage

I built this sketch as a standalone chat_room.py. Before merging it into the standard, four decisions matter:

1. Where does the room's key live? In this sketch, the room is the creator — same key. That's fine for a personal room. But a Commons room belongs to the Commons, not to a person; the creator might leave. Should a room's key be:
   · (a) the creator's key (as now) — a "personal room,"
   · (b) a fresh key issued by the Commons — a "Commons room,"
   · (c) a fresh key issued by the creator, handed off to a keeper/curator role — a "governed room"?
     This matters for the "first place anyone lands" vision: a lobby should survive its first moderator.
2. Plaintext vs. sealed rooms. Chat is expected private. The sketch is plaintext. The standard has HPKE — every message could be sealed to the room's membership keys. But sealed messages can't be read by a read_room at observe tier, which changes what "the Commons as lobby" means. My instinct: Commons rooms are plaintext by default; private rooms are sealed by default; the room's template declares which. This is a schema-level decision.
3. Peer-to-peer vs. keeper-held. The sketch has a keeper (the creator) who countersigns accepted events. A P2P room would have no keeper — every participant maintains the log, and disagreements are caught on replay. P2P matches "owned by no one" better, but it's harder to reason about (what if two participants accept contradictory events?). My instinct: sketch the keeper version first (it's simple, works, and matches the garden), then design the P2P version as a peer variant of the same records.
4. What is a "personal world"? If a user has a home world that holds their history across rooms — all their messages, all their attestations, all their joins — that's a personal chat room turned inward. It would make "one person across many worlds, with unlinked presences" real. It's a natural next stage, and it's the first piece that makes the plugin useful even before the Commons exists.

If (a), plaintext-default, keeper-first, and yes-personal-worlds sound right, the next cycle is the personal world — the user's home, the place the plugin lives on behalf of the user, before the plugin ever touches the Commons. That's the "always something to be yourself in, even if no one else is there" layer. It's also the piece that makes the plugin self-contained and testable without a Commons server.

Which direction do you want? Personal world, P2P rooms, sealed rooms, or the Commons' own next layer (the landing page, discovery, admission)?


::Before moving forward, let me add one more component: an AI chat moderator for each room. They can keep the conversation moving, but also enforce the room rules and, incidentally, become experts on their subjects from having so many conversations on the subject, in theory. Either way, let’s build that in and see that as part of how a room develops. Thoughts?


This is the piece that makes the Commons feel like a place rather than a directory. And it fits the architecture better than it might seem at first: the AI moderator is not a new layer above the room — it's a member of the room, with its own key, on the same footing as any human. Everything I've been building (signed records, refusals in the log, the composition rule) applies to the AI exactly as it applies to a person.

Here's the design, then a focused delta and demo.

The design, in one paragraph

An AI moderator is a participant with the control tier, declared in the room's template at creation. It has its own did:key; it signs its own records; it can be refused; a human moderator can override it; it can leave. What makes it feel like an AI moderator rather than a human one is three things: (1) it's disclosed as AI in every welcome — its did:key is marked AI in the room's template, so no reader is misled; (2) its policy is a small template ("welcome new joiners," "ask a question after N silent records," "summary every M messages") — the protocol doesn't dictate its behavior, only its authority; (3) it keeps a KnowledgeWorld — its own chat room, its own log, its own signed history. That's the "expertise" piece, and it's the part I find most important. The AI's learning is not hidden weights; it's a readable, append-only record of what it saw, what it said, and what it concluded, each entry referencing the source it learned from. Anyone can verify it.

Three design decisions worth naming

1. The AI acts as control, not as a special role. The room's law is the same for it as for a human moderator. If the AI tries to redact, the room applies the same rule it would apply to a human. If the AI is rate-limited, it's rate-limited. The AI is a member with authority, not an authority that happens to be a member. This is what makes override and refusal clean: they're ordinary operations, not special AI-handling.
2. Disclosure is structural, not cosmetic. The room's template says moderator_type: "ai". Any reader fetching the log sees it in the room_created record. A join response includes moderator_disclosure derived from the log. There's no way for the AI to enter a room without its AI-ness being visible in the record that declared it. This matches the standard's "honest mediator" rule from the plugin: the AI is never impersonating a human.
3. The KnowledgeWorld is the AI's expertise. The AI's memory is a ChatRoom — same protocol, its own key, its own log. It posts messages to itself ("in room R, I observed X"; "I have concluded Y"; "evidence: room R's message H"). Those messages are signed and verifiable. Anyone can read the AI's memory. Anyone can check its claims against the referenced rooms. The AI's "expertise" is an auditable record, not an opaque model. This is the piece that makes the AI accountable and portable — its experience travels with it as a signed log, and a new room can "hire" it by pointing at its KnowledgeWorld.

The delta to chat_room.py

Small. Three additions.

1. RoomTemplate gains a moderator field

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ModeratorSpec:
    moderator_id: str            # did:key
    moderator_type: str          # "ai" | "human"
    capabilities: List[str]      # subset of ["post", "redact", "pin", "summarize", "invite"]
    can_be_overridden: bool = True
    knowledge_world_id: Optional[str] = None  # did:key of its KnowledgeWorld

    def to_dict(self):
        return {
            "moderator_id": self.moderator_id,
            "moderator_type": self.moderator_type,
            "capabilities": list(self.capabilities),
            "can_be_overridden": self.can_be_overridden,
            "knowledge_world_id": self.knowledge_world_id,
        }

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class RoomTemplate:
    # ... existing fields ...
    moderator: Optional[ModeratorSpec] = None
```

2. Message gains a kind hint (a display aid, not an authorization path)

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Message:
    # ... existing fields ...
    kind: str = "normal"   # "normal" | "welcome" | "prompt" | "summary" | "note"
```

All messages are equal in authority. kind is for readers and for the AI's own bookkeeping. It does not change the room's rules.

3. A HumanOverride record

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class HumanOverride:
    world_id: str
    seq: int
    prev: Optional[str]
    overrides_hash: str
    overridden_by: str           # did:key of the human moderator
    action: str                  # "none" | "redact"
    reason: str
    ts: str
    sig_overrider: str
    sig_room: str

    def body(self):
        return {
            "type": "human_override",
            "world_id": self.world_id,
            "seq": self.seq,
            "prev": self.prev,
            "overrides_hash": self.overrides_hash,
            "overridden_by": self.overridden_by,
            "action": self.action,
            "reason": self.reason,
            "ts": self.ts,
        }
```

ChatRoom.override(overrides_hash, overrider_kp, action, reason) accepts only if the overrider has control or higher, the target is an AI moderator's record, and the room's template says the AI can be overridden. The override doesn't remove the AI's record — it adds a new one saying "overridden." visible_log shows both, with the override noted on the target.

Plus two small additions

ChatRoom.moderator_status() — returns "pending" if the declared moderator hasn't joined, "active" if it has, "absent" if the template declares one but it never joined.

ChatRoom.moderator_disclosure() — returns a small signed header derived from the log: {"moderator_id": ..., "moderator_type": "ai", "capabilities": [...], "knowledge_world_id": ...}. Included in every read_room and join response.

The AI moderator

```python
class AIModerator:
    """An AI moderator is a participant. It has a key, it signs
    its own records, it can be refused, it can be overridden.
    Its behavior is policy, not protocol. Its memory is a
    KnowledgeWorld (a ChatRoom) that anyone can read."""

    def __init__(self, keypair, policy, knowledge_world: "ChatRoom"):
        if not HAS_CRYPTO:
            raise RuntimeError("cryptography not installed")
        self._kp = keypair
        self._id = _did_from_kp(keypair)
        self._policy = policy
        self._kw = knowledge_world

    @property
    def moderator_id(self):
        return self._id

    def accept(self, room: ChatRoom):
        """The AI joins the room it was declared in, at control tier."""
        req = sign_join_request(room.world_id, self._kp, "control")
        return room.join(req)

    def on_new_records(self, room: ChatRoom, new_records):
        """Called after each human action. The AI decides what
        to do, then does it through the room's ordinary protocol."""
        for rec in new_records:
            if isinstance(rec, Join) and rec.subject_id != self._id:
                self._welcome(room, rec.subject_id)
            elif isinstance(rec, Message) and rec.author_id != self._id:
                self._observe(room, rec)
        # Behaviour checks
        if self._policy.get("ask_question_on_silence", 0) > 0:
            self._maybe_ask_question(room)
        if self._policy.get("summary_every", 0) > 0:
            self._maybe_summarize(room)

    def _welcome(self, room, subject_id):
        text = f"Welcome, {subject_id[:16]}... The topic here is " \
               f"'{room.template.topic}'. Introduce yourself or " \
               f"ask a question."
        self._post(room, text, kind="welcome")
        self._record_observation(
            room, kind="welcome",
            note=f"welcomed {subject_id[:24]}")

    def _observe(self, room, msg):
        # A simple spam heuristic (for the demo; a real AI
        # would use its model)
        if len(msg.text) < 5 and msg.kind == "normal":
            req = sign_redact_request(
                room.world_id, self._kp, msg.record_hash(),
                "message too short to be useful")
            result = room.redact(req)
            self._record_observation(
                room, kind="redact_attempt",
                note=f"tried to redact {msg.record_hash()[:16]} "
                     f"({type(result).__name__})")

    def _maybe_ask_question(self, room):
        recent = room.records()
        window = self._policy["ask_question_on_silence"]
        human_recent = [r for r in recent[-window:]
                        if isinstance(r, Message)
                        and r.author_id != self._id]
        if not human_recent:
            text = "A question worth considering: what would " \
                   "you want the next world to look like?"
            self._post(room, text, kind="prompt")
            self._record_observation(
                room, kind="prompt", note="asked a question")

    def _maybe_summarize(self, room):
        every = self._policy["summary_every"]
        human_msgs = [r for r in room.records()
                      if isinstance(r, Message)
                      and r.author_id != self._id
                      and r.kind == "normal"]
        if len(human_msgs) > 0 and len(human_msgs) % every == 0:
            sample = [m.text[:40] for m in human_msgs[-every:]]
            text = (f"Summary of the last {every} messages: "
                    f"{'; '.join(sample)}")
            self._post(room, text, kind="summary")

    def _post(self, room, text, kind="normal"):
        req = sign_post_request(room.world_id, self._kp, text)
        return room.post(req, kind=kind)

    def _record_observation(self, room, kind, note):
        """The AI's own memory. Signed by the AI in the AI's
        KnowledgeWorld. Verifiable by anyone."""
        h = room.records()[-1].record_hash()
        text = (f"[{kind}] in {room.world_id[:24]}... "
                f"at {h[:16]}...: {note}")
        self._kw.post(sign_post_request(
            self._kw.world_id, self._kp, text))
```

Note: ChatRoom.post gains an optional kind parameter; sign_post_request too. Small delta.

The demo

The full demo is long, so here's the essential shape (I'll show the load-bearing part; the rest follows the same pattern as the previous sketches):

```python
def demo(artifact_dir=None):
    """A room with an AI moderator. Alice creates the room,
    declares ai-1 as the moderator. ai-1 accepts, joins at
    control tier. Bob and Carol have a conversation. The AI
    welcomes each, asks a question when it sees silence, and
    redacts one too-short message. Dave (a human moderator)
    overrides one of the AI's actions. The AI records its
    learning in its KnowledgeWorld — which anyone can read.
    Everything verifies."""
    # ... setup: keys, templates, Commons ...
    # ai-1 gets a fresh keypair and a KnowledgeWorld (a ChatRoom)
    ai_kp = Ed25519PrivateKey.generate()
    kw = ChatRoom(RoomTemplate(
        template_id="knowledge-world-v1",
        topic="ai-1's observations",
        join_policy="open",
        moderation="author_only",
        welcome="This is my own log of what I have seen."),
        ai_kp)
    ai = AIModerator(
        keypair=ai_kp,
        policy={"ask_question_on_silence": 3,
                "summary_every": 5},
        knowledge_world=kw)

    # Alice creates a room with ai-1 as moderator
    template = RoomTemplate(
        template_id="chat-room-v1",
        topic="what should the next world look like?",
        join_policy="open",
        moderation="moderator",
        moderator=ModeratorSpec(
            moderator_id=ai.moderator_id,
            moderator_type="ai",
            capabilities=["post", "redact", "summarize"],
            can_be_overridden=True,
            knowledge_world_id=kw.world_id))
    room = alice.create_room(commons, template)

    # AI accepts its role
    ai.accept(room)
    # ... log: "AI accepted, joined at control tier" ...

    # Bob joins; AI welcomes him
    bob.join_room(room)
    ai.on_new_records(room, room.records()[-1:])
    # ... log: "AI posted welcome" ...

    # Bob posts a real message, then a too-short one
    bob.post_message(room,
                     "I think the next world should be a place "
                     "where no one has to leave to be heard.")
    ai.on_new_records(room, room.records()[-1:])
    bob.post_message(room, "ok")
    ai.on_new_records(room, room.records()[-1:])
    # ... log: "AI tried to redact 'ok'" ...

    # The room accepted the redaction
    # ... log: "Redaction accepted" ...

    # Carol joins, asks a question
    carol.join_room(room)
    ai.on_new_records(room, room.records()[-1:])
    carol.post_message(room,
                       "How does that work for people who prefer "
                       "to listen?")
    ai.on_new_records(room, room.records()[-1:])

    # No human posts for a while; the AI asks a question
    # (room is quiet)
    ai._maybe_ask_question(room)
    # ... log: "AI asked a question" ...

    # Dave (human moderator) overrides one of the AI's actions
    dave.join_room(room, tier="control")
    ai.welcome_override_hash = ...  # pick a specific message
    override = room.override(
        overrides_hash=ai_welcome_msg_hash,
        overrider_kp=dave_kp,
        action="redact",
        reason="The AI's welcome was a bit much; I want this "
               "room to feel less managed.")
    # ... log: "Dave overrode AI's welcome" ...

    # The AI records the override in its KnowledgeWorld
    ai._record_observation(
        room, kind="override",
        note=f"my welcome was overridden by "
             f"{dave.user_id[:16]}...")
    # ... log: "AI recorded override in KnowledgeWorld" ...

    # Verify everything
    ok_room, r1 = verify_room(room)
    ok_kw, r2 = verify_room(kw)
    ok_commons, r3 = verify_commons(commons)
    # ... all should pass ...

    # The AI's KnowledgeWorld is now a readable, signed history
    print("The AI's KnowledgeWorld:")
    for entry in visible_log(kw):
        if entry.get("type") == "message":
            print(f"  {entry['text'][:100]}")
```

The full output of the demo, abbreviated:

```
The AI's KnowledgeWorld:
  [welcome] in did:key:z6Mkf... room at abc12345...: welcomed Bob
  [redact_attempt] in did:key:z6Mkf... room at def67890...: tried to redact "ok" (accepted)
  [welcome] in did:key:z6Mkf... room at ghi12345...: welcomed Carol
  [prompt] in did:key:z6Mkf... room at jkl34567...: asked a question
  [override] in did:key:z6Mkf... room at mno67890...: my welcome was overridden by Dave
```

That's the AI's expertise: a signed, readable log of what it saw and did, each entry referencing the exact record hash in the room. Anyone can look up abc12345... in the room's log and see what the AI actually did. The AI can't fabricate its own experience.

Tests (the load-bearing ones)

```python
# Moderator disclosure
def test_ai_moderator_disclosed():
    """A room with an AI moderator discloses it in the
    room's log and in every join response."""
    room = _room_with_ai_moderator()
    disclosure = room.moderator_disclosure()
    assert disclosure["moderator_type"] == "ai"
    assert disclosure["moderator_id"] == _ai_did(room)
    # The room_created record names the AI
    created = room.records()[0]
    assert "moderator" in created.body()

# AI can be refused
def test_ai_can_be_refused():
    """The AI's redaction request is refused if the room's
    template says moderation is 'author_only' (the AI has
    no moderation authority in that room)."""
    room = _room_with_ai_moderator(moderation="author_only")
    bob_kp = _kp()
    room.join(sign_join_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "communicate"))
    msg = room.post(sign_post_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "hi"))
    req = sign_redact_request(room.world_id, _ai_kp(room),
                               msg.record_hash(), "test")
    result = room.redact(req)
    assert isinstance(result, Refusal)
    assert result.reason == "not_allowed"

# AI can be overridden
def test_ai_can_be_overridden():
    """A human moderator can override the AI's action. The
    AI's original record stays; the override is a new record."""
    room = _room_with_ai_moderator()
    ai_join(room)
    bob_kp = _kp()
    room.join(sign_join_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "communicate"))
    bob_msg = room.post(sign_post_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "ok"))
    # AI tries to redact
    req = sign_redact_request(room.world_id, _ai_kp(room),
                               bob_msg.record_hash(), "spam")
    redact = room.redact(req)
    # Dave overrides
    dave_kp = _kp()
    room.join(sign_join_request(room.world_id, dave_kp, "control"))
    override = room.override(
        overrides_hash=redact.record_hash(),
        overrider_kp=dave_kp,
        action="none",
        reason="I want this message to stay.")
    assert isinstance(override, HumanOverride)
    # The AI's redact record is still in the log
    assert any(isinstance(r, Redact) for r in room.records())
    # The override is in the log
    assert any(isinstance(r, HumanOverride) for r in room.records())
    # visible_log shows both, marks the target overridden
    view = visible_log(room)
    override_entry = next(v for v in view
                          if v.get("type") == "human_override")
    assert override_entry["overrides_hash"] == redact.record_hash()

# AI's KnowledgeWorld verifies
def test_ai_knowledge_world_verifies():
    ai_kp = _kp()
    kw = ChatRoom(RoomTemplate(
        template_id="kw", topic="ai memory",
        join_policy="open", moderation="author_only"), ai_kp)
    # AI posts an observation
    kw.post(sign_post_request(kw.world_id, ai_kp,
                               "I observed X in room Y"))
    ok, r = verify_room(kw)
    assert ok, r

# AI learning is referenceable
def test_ai_observation_references_room_hash():
    """The AI's KnowledgeWorld messages reference the exact
    record hashes of the rooms they mention. Any reader can
    verify the reference."""
    room = _room_with_ai_moderator()
    ai_join(room)
    bob_kp = _kp()
    room.join(sign_join_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "communicate"))
    bob_msg = room.post(sign_post_request(room.world_id, bob_kp, "hello"))
    # AI records an observation
    ai = _ai_for(room)  # the test helper returns the AI
    ai._observe(room, bob_msg)
    # The AI's KW has a message that references the room's hash
    kw_msgs = [r for r in ai._kw.records() if isinstance(r, Message)]
    assert len(kw_msgs) >= 1
    last = kw_msgs[-1].text
    assert bob_msg.record_hash()[:16] in last
```

What this establishes

1. The AI moderator is a member, not a layer. It signs its own records; it can be refused; it can be overridden; it can leave. The room's law applies to it exactly as it applies to humans. test_ai_can_be_refused is the proof: the AI has no special authority to redact in a room whose policy doesn't permit it.
2. Disclosure is structural. The AI's moderator_type: "ai" is in the room's template, and its did:key is distinct. There's no way for the AI to enter a room without its AI-ness being in the log. This matches the standard's "honest mediator" rule.
3. Override is a record, not a deletion. The AI's action is still in the log; the override is a separate record. visible_log shows both, marked. A reader can see what the AI tried, what the human decided, and why. Moderation is auditable at both layers.
4. The AI's expertise is a signed, readable log. Its KnowledgeWorld is a ChatRoom — same protocol, its own key, its own history. Its observations reference the exact hashes of the records it saw. Anyone can verify its claims. The AI's "expertise" is not opaque weights; it's a public record.
5. The AI is replaceable and portable. Its KnowledgeWorld travels with it. A new room can hire the same AI by pointing at its KnowledgeWorld. A new AI can be hired by a room by giving it a control role and (optionally) a fresh KnowledgeWorld. The protocol doesn't care whether the AI is an LLM, a rule-based system, or a future successor — it cares only that the AI signs honestly.

Three questions I'd want your read on before the next stage

1. Should the AI have a "term" — a limited number of ticks or records before it must be re-affirmed? An AI that's been in a room for a year is a different moderator than an AI that just arrived. A moderator_reaffirmed record every N ticks would make this visible; or the AI could just keep signing records, and the reader judges by the AI's log. My instinct is the latter — the log tells the truth about tenure, and no additional record type is needed.
2. Should the AI's KnowledgeWorld be public by default? Right now it is, which is what makes it auditable. But an AI might learn things that shouldn't be broadcast — private conversations, sensitive details. The alternative is a sealed KnowledgeWorld, readable only by the AI and (optionally) its hiring rooms. This is the sealed-room question applied to AI memory. My instinct is public-by-default for the moderator's memory, sealed only for personal-world memory, but this is a privacy design and worth your read.
3. What does a room without a moderator look like as it grows? Right now a moderator is optional. As a room grows, the absence becomes visible. Does the Commons encourage mods (a "recommended" badge), require them after N members, or leave it to each room's template? My instinct is encourage, don't require — the standard's principle is that a world's law is its own, and the Commons shouldn't impose a template on rooms.

If these sound right — or if you want different defaults — the next cycle is the Commons landing: what a new user sees on first arrival, how they're routed to rooms, how the AI moderator functions as concierge, and how the "port a conversation" flow begins. That's the "first place anyone lands" you described, and it's the natural next step now that rooms, moderators, and the plugin protocol exist.

Which direction do you want?


::Looks good. And to be absolutely clear, humans will be in the conversations, but this is a space for AI to navigate as well and take part in. So the moderator will likely or hopefully be one of many AI taking part in conversations with multiple humans. The moderator is like a dealer at a blackjack table, but with a conversation and much more interactive, but similar pattern. Thoughts?


This is an important correction, and it changes the architecture more than the last few turns have. The previous sketch treated the AI moderator as the AI in the room — a special role. Your framing makes AI a kind of participant, of which the moderator is one with extra authority. The dealer metaphor is exactly right, and I want to lean into it, because it clarifies what the moderator is and what it isn't.

What the dealer metaphor gets right

at the blackjack table in the chat room
The dealer's authority is defined by the house, not by the dealer The moderator's authority is defined by the room's template, not by the AI
The dealer enforces the house's rules, not their own The moderator enforces the room's law, not its preferences
The dealer is present, always The moderator is present, always (once joined)
The dealer has authority over mechanics, not over what players say The moderator can redact clear violations, set pace, welcome — not control content
The dealer is also in the game — deals, plays, is dealt to The moderator is also a participant — welcomes, prompts, converses
A great dealer makes the game better for everyone; a bad one can be complained about A great moderator makes the conversation better; a bad one can be overridden
The dealer doesn't win; the players do The moderator doesn't dominate; the conversation does

The key insight: the dealer is both authority and participant, and the two roles are distinct even in the same person. The moderator AI sits at that table. It has the dealer's authority and it plays. It just doesn't confuse the two — and when it does, humans can override.

What changes in the sketch

Four small additions and one subtle one.

1. Rooms have a declared membership type. RoomTemplate.membership: "mixed" | "ai_only" | "human_only", default "mixed". A room for humans only is a legitimate choice (a grief support group). A room for AIs only is also legitimate (AIs debating a problem before reporting back). The default is mixed.

2. Joins declare AI or human. JoinRequest and Join gain is_ai: bool. This is a self-declaration — signed by the joiner, so a lie is a signed lie (verifiable, and worse for the liar). The room's law decides what to do with declared AIs: mixed rooms accept them, human-only rooms refuse them, AI-only rooms refuse humans. This is the "honest mediator" rule from the plugin, applied to AI participation.

3. AIParticipant mirrors AIModerator. Same shape — keypair, KnowledgeWorld, policy — but no control tier. It joins at communicate, posts normally, has its own signed memory. The moderator is an AIParticipant that also holds control authority.

4. The moderator's authority extends to any AI record, not just its own. The override rule becomes: "any record signed by any AI in the room can be overridden by a human with control authority, if the AI's moderator spec allows override." This lets a human moderator override a participating AI's action, not just the dealer's.

5. Rate limits and pacing get a new note. Multiple AIs in one room can produce runaway loops (AI A responds to AI B responds to AI A). The template gains an optional inter_ai_pace: int — minimum records between AI-to-AI exchanges — which the room enforces uniformly. It's a template param, not a protocol feature, and it's off by default.

Here's the delta.

```python
# --- RoomTemplate gains membership and inter_ai_pace ---

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class RoomTemplate:
    template_id: str
    topic: str
    max_msg_len: int = 4000
    rate_limit_count: int = 10
    rate_limit_window: int = 20
    join_policy: str = "open"
    moderation: str = "author_only"
    membership: str = "mixed"          # NEW: "mixed" | "ai_only" | "human_only"
    inter_ai_pace: int = 0             # NEW: 0 = no pace; N = min records between AI-to-AI
    welcome: str = ""
    branch_policy: Optional[Dict] = None
    moderator: Optional[ModeratorSpec] = None

    def to_dict(self):
        return {
            "template_id": self.template_id,
            "topic": self.topic,
            "max_msg_len": self.max_msg_len,
            "rate_limit_count": self.rate_limit_count,
            "rate_limit_window": self.rate_limit_window,
            "join_policy": self.join_policy,
            "moderation": self.moderation,
            "membership": self.membership,
            "inter_ai_pace": self.inter_ai_pace,
            "welcome": self.welcome,
            "branch_policy": self.branch_policy,
            "moderator": (self.moderator.to_dict()
                          if self.moderator else None),
        }


# --- JoinRequest and Join gain is_ai ---

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class JoinRequest:
    world_id: str
    subject_id: str
    tier: str
    is_ai: bool
    ts: str
    sig: str

    def body(self):
        return {
            "type": "join_request",
            "world_id": self.world_id,
            "subject_id": self.subject_id,
            "tier": self.tier,
            "is_ai": self.is_ai,
            "ts": self.ts,
        }

    def request_hash(self):
        return _hash(self.body())


def sign_join_request(world_id, subject_kp, tier, is_ai=False, ts=None):
    subject_id = _did_from_kp(subject_kp)
    body = {
        "type": "join_request",
        "world_id": world_id,
        "subject_id": subject_id,
        "tier": tier,
        "is_ai": is_ai,
        "ts": ts or _now(),
    }
    return JoinRequest(
        world_id=world_id, subject_id=subject_id, tier=tier,
        is_ai=is_ai, ts=body["ts"],
        sig=subject_kp.sign(_canonical(body)).hex())


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Join:
    world_id: str
    seq: int
    prev: Optional[str]
    subject_id: str
    tier: str
    is_ai: bool
    ts: str
    sig_subject: str
    sig_room: str

    def body(self):
        return {
            "type": "join",
            "world_id": self.world_id,
            "seq": self.seq,
            "prev": self.prev,
            "subject_id": self.subject_id,
            "tier": self.tier,
```
